```tsx
    const top = node.getBoundingClientRect().top - listRef.current.getBoundingClientRect().top;
    listRef.current.scrollTop = listRef.current.scrollTop + top - ANCHOR_OFFSET_PX;
  }, [anchorId, items.length, listRef]);
}
```

**Focus management — the accessibility half of navigation.** Restoring scroll is useless if focus stays on a link in a removed subtree (then Tab starts from the top of the document, or from the body). Rules: on route change, move focus to the new page's `<h1>` (or a `[data-route-focus]` element) with `tabIndex={-1}`; on POP, restore focus to the element that had it; announce the new page with a polite live region for screen readers; and never remove the focused element without moving focus (focus falls to `<body>` and is lost). Modal routes (`?modal=`) must trap focus and restore it to the trigger on close (4.5.8).

**Trade-offs.** Restoring by key is simple and works for stable lists; it needs `history.state` or a ref map and it does not survive a reload (sessionStorage does). Anchored restoration is correct and works with mutations and virtualisation; it costs a fetch of the containing page and a measure-after-render step. Programmatic navigation with `state` is convenient but the state is lost on reload and cannot hold large data (structured-clone limits, and it ends up in the history entry visible to the user). Relative navigation (`..`) is refactor-safe but confusing when read literally — prefer it inside nested layouts.

**Common mistakes.** Scrolling to top on POP (users hate it). Scrolling on REPLACE. Saving scroll only on unmount (misses in-page navigation). Restoring before the data arrives. Focus lost after navigation. Using `navigate` inside a mount effect without a guard. Putting data in `navigate(state)`.

**What an interviewer probes.**
- "What is `useNavigationType` for and why does scroll policy depend on it?"
- "Why do pixel offsets fail for restoration?"
- "How do you restore position in a virtualised, mutable list?"
- "Where should focus go after a route change?"
- "`push` vs `replace` — when?"

**Mistakes:** restoring scroll on PUSH; no focus management; scroll restoration before data arrives; `navigate` in an unguarded effect.

---

## 4.5.8 Parallel routes, intercepting routes, and modal routing

⚫ Expert · **Must-know** · Prerequisites: 4.5.3 (nesting), 4.5.7 (focus), 4.2.1 (App Router conventions)

**Definition:** **Parallel routes** render multiple independent route slots into the *same layout at the same time* — so one layout can host, say, a feed and an authenticated sidebar that load and refresh independently. **Intercepting routes** render one slot *in place of* its normal position when the navigation came from a specific origin (a `<Link>` from the feed, a form submission) while falling back to the normal position on a hard navigation (a direct URL load, a refresh, a paste). Together they give you the "modal routing" pattern that makes a photo lightbox feel instant while keeping the URL shareable and the deep link correct.

**Why it exists — the problem it solves.** A photo lightbox done naively has two bad options: (a) render a modal without changing the URL — fast and pretty, but not shareable and Back closes the whole app; (b) navigate to `/photos/42` and render a full page — shareable, but the feed is destroyed and you lose the instant feel. Intercepting routes give you *both*: the URL becomes `/photos/42` (shareable, Back works, SSR/hard-load correct) **and** the transition from the feed is a local render with the feed still mounted behind the modal.

**Mechanics in Next.js App Router** (the canonical implementation):

```
app/
  layout.tsx                 // the ROOT layout: html/body
  @feed/                     // a PARALLEL SLOT — rendered into <Feed> in layout.tsx
    default.tsx              // fallback for the slot when it can't be rendered on this navigation (REQUIRED)
    page.tsx
  @modal/                    // a second parallel slot
    default.tsx
    (.)photos/[id]/page.tsx  // (.) = intercept from the SAME segment level
    photos/[id]/page.tsx     // hmm — the real pair is usually:
  photos/[id]/page.tsx       // the real route: renders FULL PAGE on a hard load / refresh
  photos/page.tsx            // the feed (or gallery) route

// (.)  same level        (..)  one level above        (...)  from the root app directory
// Matching rules for an intercepted route:
//  · Soft navigation from the matching origin  -> render the INTERCEPTED route (inside the slot)
//  · Hard navigation (first load, refresh, paste, external link) -> render the REAL route full page
```

```tsx
// Root layout declares the slots. Layouts RECEIVE slot props; they do not render slot components themselves.
export default function RootLayout({ children, feed, modal }: {
  children: React.ReactNode;      // the implicit `children` slot (the main content)
  feed: React.ReactNode;          // @feed
  modal: React.ReactNode;         // @modal
}) {
  return (
    <html lang="en">
      <body>
        {feed}                     // independent slot: its own loading/error boundary, refetches on its own
        {children}
        {modal}                    // the intercepted modal renders here on soft navigation
      </body>
    </html>
  );
}
```

```tsx
// The modal route itself: it must behave differently on a hard load (render a real page) vs a soft one
// (render a dialog over the feed). You detect that with the router or by checking the navigation type.
'use client';
import { useRouter } from 'next/navigation';
import { Dialog } from './Dialog';

export default function PhotoModal({ params }: { params: { id: string } }) {
  const router = useRouter();
  const isSoftNavigation = typeof window !== 'undefined' && performance.getEntriesByType('navigation')[0]?.type !== 'reload';
  // More robust: the frame is rendered inside the @modal slot only when interception happened, so the
  // component can simply check whether it is inside a dialog context provided by the layout.
  return (
    <Dialog open onClose={() => router.back()}      // Back, not 'replace to the feed': Back must close the modal
                                                     // and land on the feed, and the feed must still be there.
      label={`Photo ${params.id}`}>
      <img src={`/photos/${params.id}/full`} alt="" />
    </Dialog>
  );
}
// Rules that make modal routing correct:
//  · Close with history.back() (or a `replace` to the underlying page) so the URL and history stay coherent.
//  · A "close" must restore focus to the trigger (4.5.7) and be reachable by Escape.
//  · When the route is entered by a HARD navigation, `default.tsx` in @modal renders instead, so the user
//    gets a full page (shareable, indexable) — this is the fallback that makes the pattern safe.
//  · The underlying slot must not re-fetch when the modal opens, or you get a visible refetch flash behind the dialog.
```

**The React Router / Remix equivalent** (no parallel slots; you get the same UX with a URL convention):

```tsx
// Convention: a modal is a route with a `?modal=` (or a `layout` sibling) that renders over the current view.
// React Router's nested-routes + location.state gives you interception semantics manually:
const routes = [
  { path: '/gallery', element: <GalleryLayout />, children: [
      { index: true, element: <Gallery /> },
      // The "intercepted" variant lives at the SAME path but reads the URL marker.
      { path: 'photo/:id', element: <PhotoRoute /> },
  ]},
];

function GalleryLayout() {
  const location = useLocation();
  const params = useParams();
  const isModal = new URLSearchParams(location.search).get('modal') === 'photo';
  // Soft nav: /gallery?modal=photo&photo=42   -> render <PhotoRoute> as a dialog, gallery still mounted
  // Hard nav: /gallery/photo/42              -> render <PhotoRoute> as a full page
  return (
    <>
      <Outlet />
      {isModal && <Dialog onClose={() => navigate(-1)}><PhotoRoute id={params.id} /></Dialog>}
    </>
  );
}

// Progressively enhanced, no-JS form of a modal route: <Form method="get" action="/gallery?modal=photo" />
// works because GET forms navigate the same way a Link does.
```

**Why `default.tsx` (parallel-route fallback) is mandatory** — this is the interview-worthy detail. A parallel slot must render *something* on every navigation that does not target it. Without a `default.tsx`, a soft navigation to a route outside the slot's match throws "missing default.js / notFound". The fallback content should be a cheap placeholder (a skeleton, or nothing at all) — not a duplicate of the real content, because then you fetch twice.

**Trade-offs.** Parallel routes buy independent loading/error boundaries and independent refresh semantics inside one layout — genuinely useful for dashboards (a slow "usage" widget must not block the feed) and for authenticated chrome. They cost: the slot props are unusual and easy to get wrong, the fallback requirement is easy to miss, and debuggers show duplicated fetches when a slot renders on a navigation it should not have. Intercepting routes buy the best modal UX in web apps; they cost the most complex routing mental model in any framework, two code paths per modal (soft and hard), and the requirement that every modal be a *real route* with its own data loading — which is more work than a component, and exactly why it is worth it (the alternative is a modal that cannot be shared or server-rendered).

**When not to use them.** Don't intercept if the "modal" is genuinely transient (a confirm dialog, a tooltip, a zoom — use a component and a portal; 4.1.16). Don't use parallel slots for two things that always load together — use `Promise.all` in one component and one Suspense boundary; slots are for *independent* lifecycles. Don't use `@slot` for something that is not part of the same URL (it is not addressable; if it needs an address, it is a route).

**Production and operational.** Give every slot its own `loading.tsx`/`error.tsx` so one failing slot does not blank the layout. Watch for double-fetching across slots (two slots reading the same entity: use the query cache to dedupe, or fetch once in the layout and pass down). Track modal opens in analytics *with* the navigation type, because "share a photo URL, land in the modal" vs "land in the full page" is a real product decision you should be measuring. Make sure the intercepted route has a real, indexable full-page equivalent — otherwise you have built a URL that crawlers and link unfurlers cannot render.

**Common mistakes.** Missing `default.tsx`. Closing a modal with `router.push('/gallery')` instead of `back()` (breaks Back). A modal with no full-page fallback. Focus not restored on close. Escape not handled. Slot content duplicated across slots (double fetch). Intercepting a route that should have been a real page.

**What an interviewer probes.**
- "What problem do intercepting routes solve that a component modal cannot?"
- "Why does a parallel slot need a default file?"
- "How do you close an intercepted modal correctly with history?"
- "When would you *not* use parallel routes?"

**Mistakes:** `push` instead of `back` on close; missing slot fallbacks; using interception for simple dialogs; duplicated data fetching across slots.

---

## 4.5.9 Deep linking, URL design, and query-param state

🟡 Intermediate · **Must-know** · Prerequisites: 4.3.8 (URL state), 4.2.11 (metadata)

**Definition:** Deep linking is the ability to open any state of the app by URL alone, on a cold load, in a new tab, shared with someone else, months later. URL design is deciding which state belongs in the path, which in the query string, which in neither, and how to make all of it canonical.

**The URL contract — the design decisions, in order.**

```
/teams/acme/projects/42/tasks?status=open&assignee=me&sort=-due&page=3&view=board&q=login%20bug
 │        │        │       │       │                                                    │
 │        │        │       │       └─ transient view state      → query string           │  filter text:
 │        │        │       │                                     (replace, canonical)   │  put it in the
 │        │        │       └─ sub-resource identity              → path segment          │  query, debounced
 │        │        └─ resource identity        → path (shareable, indexable, crawlable)
 │        └─ a resource in a collection        → path
 └─ the tenant/workspace                     → path (multi-tenant, canonical for SEO)
```

Rules that follow, each of which you should be able to justify:

1. **Identity in the path, view state in the query.** The path says *what exists*; the query says *how you are looking at it*. A URL in the query should never be the only way to identify a resource (an id in the query can be shared incorrectly and is invisible to crawlers).
2. **The URL must decide what the server returns.** `?page=3` must fetch page 3 server-side, or a shared link shows page 1 (the classic "deep link loads the wrong page" bug). Similarly for filters and sorting on any indexable page.
3. **Canonicalise.** Omit defaults (`?page=1` → `/tasks`), normalise parameter order, one canonical spelling of a filter (`status=open` not `statuses=open&open=true`), and redirect non-canonical URLs with a 301/308 so link equity and analytics do not split. Never have two URLs render identical content.
4. **One spelling per thing, forever.** Renaming a path segment means a redirect map (`old → new`, permanent) — do it once, keep it forever.
5. **URLs are a public API.** They appear in emails, support tickets, QR codes, printed receipts, and Slack messages. They must be readable, stable, and free of secrets (4.6.9). Human-readable slugs for public content (`/blog/optimistic-updates-rollback`) with a numeric id hidden behind them.
6. **Pagination state is URL state**, and its format is a product decision: page numbers for admin tables (`?page=3`), cursor for feeds (`?after=…` — visible but ugly; some products keep the cursor out of the URL and accept losing back-navigation within a feed). Be explicit about which you chose and why.
7. **Tabs and wizards**: `?tab=history` is deep-linkable (good for support and for emails linking to "the invoices tab"); a wizard step is usually URL state too (`/checkout/2/payment`) so Back works and a reload does not lose progress — but that means you must validate which steps the user may reach (4.3.7/4.5.5).
8. **Sort direction**: use an explicit prefix (`sort=-due`) rather than a separate param (`dir=desc`) so a sort is one atomic value; or use `sort=due&dir=asc` if you need both — pick one and be consistent.

```tsx
// A single, reusable URL-state hook with validation, canonicalisation, and a history policy (4.3.8).
type BoardFilters = { status: 'all' | 'open' | 'blocked'; assignee: 'any' | 'me'; sort: '-due' | 'due' | '-created'; page: number; view: 'board' | 'list'; q: string };

const BoardFiltersSchema = z.object({
  status: z.enum(['all', 'open', 'blocked']).catch('all'),
  assignee: z.enum(['any', 'me']).catch('any'),
  sort: z.enum(['-due', 'due', '-created']).catch('-due'),
  page: z.coerce.number().int().min(1).catch(1),
  view: z.enum(['board', 'list']).catch('board'),
  q: z.string().max(120).catch(''),
});

export function useBoardFilters(onFilterChange?: (f: BoardFilters) => void) {
  const [params, setParams] = useSearchParams();
  const filters = useMemo(() => BoardFiltersSchema.parse(Object.fromEntries(params)), [params]);

  const set = useCallback((patch: Partial<BoardFilters>, opts: { history?: 'push' | 'replace' } = {}) => {
    const next = { ...filters, ...patch };
    if ('q' in patch || 'status' in patch || 'assignee' in patch) next.page = 1;   // changing a filter resets the page
    setParams(canonical(next), { replace: opts.history !== 'push' });             // filters replace by default
    onFilterChange?.(next);                                                        // keep the query key in sync
  }, [filters, params, setParams, onFilterChange]);

  return [filters, set] as const;
}

// Canonicalisation: omit defaults, order keys deterministically, and NEVER include undefined/''.
function canonical(f: BoardFilters): URLSearchParams {
  const sp = new URLSearchParams();
  if (f.q) sp.set('q', f.q);
  if (f.status !== 'all') sp.set('status', f.status);
  if (f.assignee !== 'any') sp.set('assignee', f.assignee);
  if (f.sort !== '-due') sp.set('sort', f.sort);
  if (f.view !== 'board') sp.set('view', f.view);
  if (f.page > 1) sp.set('page', String(f.page));
  return sp;                                   // deterministic key order => one URL per view state
}
```

**Deep-link correctness checklist.** For every URL your app can produce: (1) a cold load in a new tab renders the same view; (2) the server (or loader) fetched the data for that exact state, not the default; (3) Back and Forward produce sensible views; (4) the URL contains no secrets; (5) it is under ~100 characters and readable; (6) it renders the right `<title>` and canonical `<link>` (4.2.11); (7) an unauthenticated request redirects to login with a safe `next` and returns to the right place (4.5.5); (8) an unknown id renders a real 404 with a way forward, not an infinite spinner.

**Interaction with the query cache.** URL-derived state belongs in the query key, or the cache is wrong:

```tsx
const filters = useBoardFilters();
const { data } = useQuery({
  queryKey: ['tasks', filters],        // ← every URL-derived input is IN the key (4.4.11 blind spot #4)
  queryFn: () => api.get(`/teams/${teamId}/tasks?${canonical(filters)}`),
  placeholderData: keepPreviousData,   // smooth paging and filtering without a skeleton flash
});
// → Back/Forward re-renders from cache instantly (staleTime permitting) and the cache stays coherent.
// If filters were in a store instead, Back would not change them and the cache key would be stale.
```

**Trade-offs.** Rich URLs cost verbosity (a 90-character query string), require validation and canonicalisation, and force you to think about which state is durable — but they buy shareability, Back-button correctness, SSR/crawlability, analytics attribution, reduced server state (nothing to persist per user), and supportability ("open this URL and you'll see what I see"). Thin URLs (everything in memory) are faster to build and look cleaner in code, and break everything above. The middle ground most products choose: *path for identity, query for view state, ephemeral UI state (open panels, hover, focused field) nowhere.* One more consideration: search-relevant state in the query string is indexable, so if a filter is not meant to be indexed, put it in the query but `noindex` that combination — and be aware that in practice this generates crawl waste.

**Production and operational.** Emit a `pageview` analytics event on `popstate` and on push, including the query string and a `navigation_type` — and make sure a replace-heavy filter change does not inflate your pageview count (debounce or fire a separate `filters_changed` event). Keep a redirect map for every URL you have ever shipped, in a data structure the server can read (`redirects.json` → a 308 map). Monitor 404s on `/app/*` paths: a spike means either a bad link somewhere or the SPA fallback is missing (4.5.1). Ensure the login redirect round trip preserves the full URL including its query string.

**Common mistakes.** Filters in a store instead of the URL. Defaults left in the URL. Two URLs for the same view. A deep link that fetches page 1 regardless of `?page=`. Search text pushed on every keystroke. `?q=` in a path with unescaped special characters. Analytics counting a filter change as a pageview.

**What an interviewer probes.**
- "Which state goes in the URL and why?"
- "How do you make a filter deep-linkable and indexable?"
- "What is canonicalisation and why does it matter?"
- "How do you keep the query cache consistent with the URL?"
- "Your app has two URLs for the same list — why does that hurt?"

**Mistakes:** store-based filters; no canonical form; `page=1` shipped in links; unvalidated params reaching the API.

---

## 4.5.10 Router comparison: React Router, TanStack Router, Next.js App Router, Remix

🔴 Advanced · **Must-know** · Prerequisites: 4.2 (meta-frameworks), 4.5.4–4.5.9

**Definition:** A router comparison is a set of decisions about *where the route table lives*, *who owns data loading*, *how caching works*, *how much framework is imposed*, and *what you must know about rendering*. These four options are not interchangeable: two are routers you mount in your own app, two are frameworks that own the whole application.

**The comparison.**

| | **React Router 6/7** | **TanStack Router** | **Next.js App Router** | **Remix / RR7 framework mode** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Category | Library (mount in your app) | Library (mount in your app) | Framework (owns the app) | Framework (owns the app) |
| Route definition | JS objects/JSX, or `routes.ts` with types | TS-first route tree, fully type-inferred | Filesystem: `app/**/page.tsx` | Filesystem: `routes/*.tsx` + a config |
| Type safety | Good with `satisfies` + `loader` typing | **Best-in-class**: routes, params, search params, and links inferred end to end | Good (typed segments, generated types) | Good (`loader`/`action` inferred) |
| Data layer | `loader`/`action` per route; you own the client cache | `loader`; plus a first-party query integration | Server Components + Server Actions + `fetch`/cache directives | `loader`/`action`; the canonical model |
| Caching/mutations | **You own it** (TanStack Query integrates cleanly) | TanStack Query is the ecosystem default | Framework caches (`'use cache'`, `revalidateTag`, `revalidatePath`) with implicit, evolving rules | You own it; `defer`/`Await` streaming |
| Rendering modes | Whatever you render (CSR, or hydrate your own SSR) | CSR primarily; SSR via your own setup | SSR/SSG/ISR/CSR, RSC, streaming, edge | SSR by default, streaming, hydration |
| Parallel/intercepting routes | No (build it yourself) | No (build it yourself) | **Yes** (`@slot`, `(.)`, `..`) | No |
| Type/JSX requirement | Framework-agnostic (works with any React renderer) | React only | React only | React only |
| Bundle/runtime cost | Small; you bring the server | Small | Large, but you get SSR/SEO/edge/CDN with it | Medium |
| Steepest learning curve | Low | Medium (type inference pays off later) | High (implicit caching, RSC rules) | Medium-high (explicit but many conventions) |
| Escape hatch | Total — it is a router | Total, mostly | You are in a framework; escape = `use client` everywhere | Medium |

```ts
// TanStack Router: the type-inference model that is its real differentiator.
const rootRoute = createRootRoute({ component: RootLayout, notFoundComponent: NotFound });
const indexRoute = createRoute({ getParentRoute: () => rootRoute, path: '/', component: Home });

const invoiceRoute = createRoute({
  getParentRoute: () => rootRoute,
  path: '/invoices/$id',                                    // $ = dynamic param
  validateSearch: (s: Record<string, unknown>) => ({      // search params are VALIDATED and TYPED
    page: Number(s.page ?? 1),
    status: ['all', 'open', 'overdue'].includes(String(s.status)) ? (s.status as Status) : 'all',
  }),
  loader: ({ params, deps }) => fetchInvoice(params.id, deps.page, deps.status),
  errorComponent: InvoiceError,
});

const routeTree = rootRoute.addChildren([indexRoute, invoiceRoute]);
export const router = createRouter({ routeTree, defaultPreload: 'intent' });   // 'intent' = preload on hover/focus!
declare module '@tanstack/react-router' { interface Register { router: typeof router } }   // required for Link/redirect types

// The payoff, in types: navigate({ to: '/invoices/$id', params: { id }, search: { page: 2, status: 'open' } })
// is a compile error if the route, param, or search key does not exist. Loaders' deps are typed, so
// forgetting to read a search param the loader needs is a type error too.
// The cost: file-based route generation (`@tanstack/router-plugin` / vite plugin) is how big apps avoid
// maintaining the tree by hand, and the plugin/CLI is a real dependency with its own upgrade cycle.
```

```tsx
// Remix / RR7 framework mode: the loader/action model, with the escape hatches that made it popular.
import { json, redirect, defer, Form, useLoaderData, useFetcher, useNavigation } from '@remix-run/react';

// useFetcher: mutate WITHOUT a navigation — the basis of optimistic UI in a form-centric framework.
function LikeButton({ postId }: { postId: string }) {
  const fetcher = useFetcher<typeof likeAction>();
  const liked = fetcher.formData ? fetcher.formData.get('liked') === 'true' : false;
  return (
    <fetcher.Form method="post" action="/posts/$postId/like">
      <input type="hidden" name="liked" value={String(!liked)} />
      <button aria-pressed={liked}>{liked ? 'Liked' : 'Like'}</button>
      {/* fetcher.state === 'submitting' gives per-button pending state with no JS routing at all */}
    </fetcher.Form>
  );
}
// Remix's distinctive strengths: everything is a form POST (works without JS), fetcher-based optimistic UI,
// defer/Await streaming, nested routes with per-route error boundaries, and a genuinely simple data model.
// Its cost: it is a framework — you adopt its build, its file conventions, and its deployment story.
```

**How to choose, in the order a staff engineer actually uses it.**

1. **Do you need server rendering, SEO, or a CDN-cached public site?** If yes → a framework (Next.js App Router, Remix). If no → a library (React Router, TanStack Router) and you keep your own deploy story. *This single question eliminates half the options.*
2. **Are you already on Next.js?** Stay on Next.js unless you have a named, measurable blocker. Migration cost is the dominant term, and Next's weaknesses (implicit caching) are now well-documented and cacheable.
3. **Do you want compile-time route/param/search typing across a large app?** → TanStack Router. It is the strongest typing story in React routing, and `defaultPreload: 'intent'` gives you prefetching for free.
4. **Do you want maximum control and the smallest surface, and are you already rendering your own HTML or using a non-standard renderer?** → React Router. It is a router, not a framework, and it composes with anything.
5. **Do you want explicit data flow, progressive enhancement, and no magic caching?** → Remix / RR7 framework mode. Every request's data flow is visible in one file.
6. **Do you need parallel/intercepting routes (modal-over-feed at scale)?** → Next.js App Router (or build it yourself, which is real work).

**Trade-offs that bite in production.**
- **Next.js:** implicit, rule-based caching that has changed defaults across versions and is the single largest source of "why is my data stale" incidents; a client/server boundary you must reason about constantly (`'use client'` at the top of a file is a big hammer); a large runtime; and a fast-moving release cadence. Mitigation: keep data access in server components, make cache policy explicit and documented per route, and pin versions.
- **React Router:** you own the cache, the SSR strategy, the error handling, and the file conventions — which is freedom and also work. Mitigation: adopt a data layer with opinions (TanStack Query), a route-error-boundary convention, and a lazy-route policy (4.5.6).
- **TanStack Router:** the plugin/codegen is a new moving part, and the type inference is a build-time cost; in exchange you get params/search typed end to end and excellent preload/intent behaviour.
- **Remix:** its loader/action model is its greatest strength and its main constraint — you must learn to think per-request, and nested-route data dependencies take a moment to internalise.

**What an interviewer probes.**
- "Compare these four routers and tell me which you would pick for *this* product."
- "What does Next's implicit caching cost you, and how do you mitigate it?"
- "What is the strongest argument for TanStack Router?"
- "What does 'progressive enhancement' mean here, and which router gives it to you for free?"
- "When would you leave Next.js?"

**Mistakes:** choosing by popularity; ignoring who owns the cache; treating a router as if it also solved data fetching; migrating for a framework preference rather than a named defect.

---

## 4.5.11 File-based routing and route organisation

🟡 Intermediate · **Must-know** · Prerequisites: 4.5.3 (segments), 4.5.6 (splitting), 4.5.10 (router choice)

**Definition:** **File-based routing** derives the route table from the directory tree — the filesystem *is* the router configuration. Add a file, get a route; delete a file, the route is gone; move a file, the URL changes. Nothing declares a path, a layout chain, or a param list in JS, because there is no second place where the URL can be written down and drift out of sync. The important part is that a convention is only as good as its escape hatches: **route groups**, **layout files**, **dynamic segments**, **optional catch-alls**, and a documented way to opt out (`routes.ts`).

**Why it exists — the problem it solves.** Central problems in a large app: (1) the URL is written in two places — config and code — so they drift; (2) adding a route touches a shared config file, so every feature PR conflicts in the same file; (3) a param rename breaks silently because no type connects the segment to the component's props; (4) nobody can tell what URLs exist without grepping the config. A convention makes the route set *enumerable* (`ls app/`), gives the compiler something to check, and lets a plugin generate types, so a bad `navigate({ to: '/invoice/$id', params: {} })` is a build error instead of a 404 in production.

**Mechanics — Next.js App Router (segments and groups):**

```
app/
  layout.tsx                     // root layout: <html>, <body>  — required
  page.tsx                       // "/"  -> re-exports the group page
  (marketing)/                   // ROUTE GROUP: parentheses = NO url segment
    layout.tsx                   //   layout for marketing only, url unchanged
    page.tsx                     //   still "/"  (the marketing homepage)
    pricing/page.tsx             //   "/pricing"
  (app)/                         // second group, same "/" — used to keep auth layouts apart
    layout.tsx                   //   authenticated shell
    dashboard/page.tsx           //   "/dashboard"
  invoices/
    layout.tsx                   // layout for /invoices/* — does NOT remount on child nav
    page.tsx                     // "/invoices"
    loading.tsx                  // Suspense fallback for this segment
    error.tsx                    // 'use client' — error boundary for THIS segment only
    not-found.tsx                // 404 UI for this segment
    [invoiceId]/                 // dynamic segment -> "/invoices/:invoiceId"
      page.tsx                   //   params: { invoiceId: string }  (await params in Next 15)
      @details/                  // parallel slot -> rendered into the parent layout's `details` prop
        page.tsx
    [...filters]/                // CATCH-ALL  -> "/invoices/a/b/c"   (one or more segments)
      page.tsx
    [[...filters]]/              // OPTIONAL CATCH-ALL -> also matches "/invoices"  (zero or more)
      page.tsx

// URL produced by the tree above:
//   /                  /pricing              /dashboard
//   /invoices          /invoices/:id         /invoices/a/b/c
// Rules:
//  · Two files may NOT resolve to the same URL without a group to disambiguate (app/page.tsx AND (marketing)/page.tsx
//    both claim "/" -> build error). Groups exist to solve exactly this.
//  · A layout persists across navigation to its children; only its `key`-scoped state resets. Put state in a
//    layout and it survives child navigation — that is a feature (a filter panel) and a bug source (a stale cart).
//  · `loading.tsx` / `error.tsx` / `not-found.tsx` are per SEGMENT, so the blast radius of a failure is one segment.
```

**Mechanics — TanStack Router (code-first but file-generated):**

```
src/routes/
  __root.tsx                      // root route: <Outlet/> + the single error boundary + providers
  index.tsx                       // "/"
  invoices/
    route.tsx                     // this file IS the route: path, loader, validateSearch, component
    index.tsx                     // "/invoices"
    $invoiceId/
      route.tsx                   // "/invoices/$invoiceId" — $ is the param marker
  posts/
    $postId/
      edit/
        route.tsx

// route.tsx is fully typed; the plugin generates a route tree and registers it:
```

```tsx
// src/routes/invoices/$invoiceId/route.tsx
import { createFileRoute } from '@tanstack/react-router'
import { z } from 'zod'

export const Route = createFileRoute('/invoices/$invoiceId')({
  validateSearch: zodValidator(searchSchema),   // search params validated -> inferred types
  loaderDeps: ({ search }) => ({ status: search.status }),   // ONLY these deps re-run the loader
  loader: ({ params, deps, context }) =>
    context.api.getInvoice(params.invoiceId, deps.status),
  component: InvoiceRoute,
  errorComponent: RouteError,        // per-route boundary
  notFoundComponent: () => <p>No such invoice</p>,
})

function InvoiceRoute() {
  const { invoiceId } = Route.useParams()     // typed from the path — rename-proof
  const { status, page } = Route.useSearch()  // typed from the Zod schema
  const invoice = Route.useLoaderData()
  // navigate() is checked: `router.navigate({ to: '/invoices/$invoiceId', params: { invoiceId } })`
  // fails to COMPILE if the path or a param name changed.
}
```

```tsx
// vite.config.ts — the plugin is what makes the convention real (it scans, generates, and type-checks)
import { tanstackRouter } from '@tanstack/router-plugin/vite'
export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [react(), tanstackRouter({ target: 'react', autoCodeSplitting: true })],
})
// `autoCodeSplitting` also splits the bundle per route file (4.5.6) for free.
```

```tsx
// The escape hatch: keep a hand-written tree and register it explicitly, for anything the convention can't express.
import { routeTree } from './routeTree.gen'      // generated
import { rootRoute } from './routes/__root'
import { adminRoute } from './routes/admin'       // hand-written, imported by the generator's config

export function registerRoutes(router: Router) {
  router.routeTree = router.routeTree.addChildren([adminRoute])   // merged into the generated tree
}
// Rule: at most ONE place may add to the route tree, and it must be documented. Two writers = two sources of truth.
```

**Trade-offs.** File-based routing buys: no config file to merge-conflict, an enumerable route set, automatic per-route code splitting, and — when codegen is involved — compile-time checking of paths, params, and search. It costs: a build step you did not write, a learning curve for the conventions (group vs folder, `[id]` vs `:id` vs `$id`), the fact that **moving a file changes a URL** (so "just reorganise" becomes a production incident without redirects), limited expressiveness for anything irregular (deeply conditional matching, versioned APIs with a custom ranking function — which is exactly why TanStack Router lets you drop to a code-defined route, and why Next lets you write a `route.ts` handler outside the tree). The rule of thumb I give teams: **convention for the 90%, an explicit escape hatch for the 10%, and never both for the same route.**

**Production and operational.** Route renames need permanent redirects — a `redirects()` map in the router config plus 301s from the edge, tested, because the URLs are in emails, bookmarks and QR codes forever. Watch the build time: codegen over a very large app is a real cost, and a slow build blocks CI. Audit the route list in CI (`routeTree` → a snapshot file, diffed in review) so an accidental `mkdir` cannot silently publish an unprotected or unstyled route. And keep auth at the loader/edge layer (4.5.5), because "this route is in a `(private)` folder" is organisation, not enforcement.

**Common mistakes.** Two files resolving to the same URL without a group; assuming a group creates a path segment (it does not); putting request state in a layout and wondering why it survives navigation; using `(group)` for auth instead of a real check; renaming a folder without redirects; mixing generated and hand-written routes without a documented single writer; and relying on the folder name for security.

**What an interviewer probes.**
- "What do route groups do, and when do you need one?"
- "How do you keep a route rename from breaking production?"
- "What does codegen buy you that a JS route array cannot?"
- "Where do you draw the line between convention and configuration?"
- "How would you audit which routes exist and which are protected?"

**Mistakes:** treating the convention as enforcement; no redirect discipline on moves; two writers for the route tree; ignoring that optional catch-all can swallow its siblings' URLs.

---

# 4.3-4.5 MUST-IMPLEMENT-FROM-SCRATCH (12 Problems)

Write each from memory. Between them they cover every mechanism in 4.3–4.5: stores, reducers, thunks, selectors, normalization, query caches, optimistic updates, URL state, matching, guards, and forms.

### 1. A typed external store with selectors (`useSyncExternalStore`, ~60 lines)

```ts
type Listener = () => void;
export type Store<T> = {
  getState: () => T;
  setState: (u: T | ((p: T) => T), replace?: boolean) => void;
  subscribe: (l: Listener) => () => void;
};

export function createStore<T extends object>(initial: T, equals: (a: T, b: T) => boolean = Object.is): Store<T> {
  let state = initial;
  const listeners = new Set<Listener>();
  return {
    getState: () => state,
    setState(u, replace = false) {
      const next = typeof u === 'function' ? (u as (p: T) => T)(state) : u;
      if (equals(state, next)) return;                  // identity equality is the change signal
      const prev = state;
      state = next;
      if (replace) listeners.clear();                   // used by "replace with server state" after a 401
      listeners.forEach((l) => l());
      void prev;
    },
    subscribe(l) { listeners.add(l); return () => { listeners.delete(l); }; },
  };
}

// React binding: subscribe to the whole store, narrow with useSyncExternalStore's selector.
export function useStore<T extends object, S>(store: Store<T>, selector: (s: T) => S): S {
  return useSyncExternalStore(
    store.subscribe,
    () => selector(store.getState()),                   // must be pure + return a stable value when unchanged
    () => selector(store.getState()),                   // getServerSnapshot for SSR
  );
}
// Slice setters with no selector juggling:
export const useActions = <T extends object>(store: Store<T>) => store.setState;
```
*Edge cases:* `getSnapshot` must not allocate a new object each call (infinite loop) — that is why the selector must return a stable reference; `equals` lets you opt into shallow comparison for object selectors; the listener set must be copied before iterating if a listener unsubscribes during notification; `setState` during a notification is a re-entrancy hazard, so either batch or document it.

### 2. Reducer + thunk middleware + action types (~70 lines)

```ts
// A Redux-shaped store with only the parts you need to understand: dispatch -> reducer -> notify.
type Action = { type: string; payload?: unknown };
type Reducer<S, A extends Action> = (state: S | undefined, action: A) => S;

export function createAppStore<S, A extends Action>(reducer: Reducer<S, A>, initial: S, middleware: Middleware[] = []) {
  let state = initial;
  let queue: A[] = [];
  let notifying = false;
  const listeners = new Set<Listener>();
  let dispatch: Dispatch = (a: A) => a;
  const api = { getState: () => state, dispatch: (a: unknown) => dispatch(a as A) };
  const chain = middleware.reduceRight((next, mw) => mw(api)(next), (a: A) => {
    if (a.type.startsWith('@@redux/INIT')) return a;
    queue.push(a);
    if (!notifying) { notifying = true; try { while (queue.length) { const n = queue.shift()!; state = reducer(state, n); } } finally { notifying = false; } }
    listeners.forEach((l) => l());
    return a;
  });
  dispatch = chain as Dispatch;
  dispatch({ type: '@@redux/INIT' });
  return { getState: () => state, dispatch, subscribe: (l: Listener) => { listeners.add(l); return () => { listeners.delete(l); }; } };
}

// Middleware: intercept a function action (a thunk) and give it dispatch + getState.
export const thunk: Middleware = ({ dispatch, getState }) => (next) => (action) =>
  typeof action === 'function' ? action(dispatch, getState) : next(action);

// A reducer with named transitions, impossible states removed, and exhaustive checking.
type CartState = { lines: Record<string, number>; status: 'idle' | 'submitting' | 'done' | 'failed' };
type CartAction =
  | { type: 'cart/added'; sku: string; qty?: number }
  | { type: 'cart/qtySet'; sku: string; qty: number }
  | { type: 'cart/submitted' } | { type: 'cart/submitOk' } | { type: 'cart/submitFail'; error: string };

const cartReducer = (state: CartState = { lines: {}, status: 'idle' }, a: CartAction): CartState => {
  switch (a.type) {
    case 'cart/added': {
      if (state.status === 'submitting') return state;                 // guard: ignore changes mid-submit
      const qty = (state.lines[a.sku] ?? 0) + (a.qty ?? 1);
      if (qty <= 0) { const { [a.sku]: _removed, ...rest } = state.lines; return { ...state, lines: rest }; }
      return { ...state, lines: { ...state.lines, [a.sku]: qty } };
    }
    case 'cart/qtySet': return { ...state, lines: { ...state.lines, [a.sku]: a.qty } };
    case 'cart/submitted': return { ...state, status: 'submitting' };
    case 'cart/submitOk': return { ...state, status: 'done' };
    case 'cart/submitFail': return { ...state, status: 'failed' };   // store the error where the UI can show it
    default: return state;
  }
};
```
*Edge cases:* a reducer must be pure — no `Date.now()`, no `fetch`, no mutation of `action`; the middleware queue must handle dispatches that occur *during* a reducer (batched, not recursive); `@@redux/INIT` needs an early return so the reducer gets `undefined` state exactly once.

### 3. Normalized entity store with adapter-style reducers + memoised selectors (~80 lines)

```ts
export type Id = string;
export type EntityState<T> = { ids: Id[]; byId: Record<Id, T> };

export const adapter = <T extends { id: Id }>(sort?: (a: T, b: T) => number) => ({
  getInitial: (): EntityState<T> => ({ ids: [], byId: {} }),
  addOne(s: EntityState<T>, e: T): EntityState<T> {
    if (s.byId[e.id]) return s;                                     // idempotent: RTK's addOne is a no-op for existing ids
    return sort ? { ids: [...s.ids, e.id].sort((x, y) => sort(s.byId[x], s.byId[y])), byId: { ...s.byId, [e.id]: e } }
                : { ids: [...s.ids, e.id], byId: { ...s.byId, [e.id]: e } };
  },
  updateOne(s: EntityState<T>, patch: Partial<T> & { id: Id }): EntityState<T> {
    const prev = s.byId[patch.id];
    if (!prev) return s;
    const next = { ...prev, ...patch };
    return { ...s, byId: { ...s.byId, [patch.id]: next } };         // array untouched => ids reference is stable
  },
  upsertOne(s: EntityState<T>, e: T): EntityState<T> {
    return s.byId[e.id] ? adapter(sort).updateOne(s, e) : adapter(sort).addOne(s, e);
  },
  removeOne(s: EntityState<T>, id: Id): EntityState<T> {
    if (!s.byId[id]) return s;
    const { [id]: _gone, ...byId } = s.byId;
    return { ids: s.ids.filter((x) => x !== id), byId };
  },
  selectAll: (s: EntityState<T>): T[] => s.ids.map((id) => s.byId[id]).filter(Boolean),   // filter guards a torn state
  selectById: (s: EntityState<T>, id: Id): T | undefined => s.byId[id],
});

// Memoisation: a 1-entry cache keyed by the input selectors' results.
export function createSelector<In extends unknown[], Out, S>(...args: any[]): (state: S) => Out {
  const inputSel = args.filter((f) => typeof f === 'function') as Array<(s: S) => unknown>;
  const combiner = args[args.length - 1] as (...vals: unknown[]) => Out;
  let lastArgs: unknown[] | null = null;
  let lastResult: Out;
  return (state: S) => {
    const vals = inputSel.map((f) => f(state));
    if (lastArgs && vals.length === lastArgs.length && vals.every((v, i) => Object.is(v, lastArgs![i]))) return lastResult;
    lastArgs = vals;
    lastResult = combiner(...vals);
    return lastResult;
  };
}
```
*Edge cases:* `addOne` must be a no-op for an existing id (an upsert would clobber local edits); the ids array must not be recreated when only an entity changes, or every list selector recomputes; `selectAll` over a large `ids` array is O(n) per call, so memoise it; the memo cache is size 1, which is why a parameterised selector needs its own factory.

### 4. A mini query cache: hashing, dedupe, staleness, GC, invalidation (~120 lines)

```ts
type QueryKey = readonly unknown[];
export type QueryState<T> = {
  status: 'pending' | 'error' | 'success';
  data: T | undefined;
  dataUpdatedAt: number;
  error: Error | null;
  fetchStatus: 'idle' | 'fetching';
  promise: Promise<T> | null;
  observers: Set<() => void>;
};

export const hashKey = (key: QueryKey): string =>
  JSON.stringify(key, (_k, v) => (v === undefined || v === null || v === '' ? '__undef' : v));  // normalise before hashing

export function createQueryCache(opts: { staleTime?: number; gcTime?: number } = {}) {
  const staleTime = opts.staleTime ?? 0;
  const gcTime = opts.gcTime ?? 5 * 60_000;
  const cache = new Map<string, QueryState<unknown>>();
  const timers = new Map<string, ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>>();

  function notify(key: QueryKey) {
    const h = hashKey(key);
    cache.get(h)?.observers.forEach((fn) => fn());
    scheduleGc(h);
  }

  function scheduleGc(h: string) {
    clearTimeout(timers.get(h));
    if ((cache.get(h)?.observers.size ?? 0) > 0) return;
    timers.set(h, setTimeout(() => { if ((cache.get(h)?.observers.size ?? 0) === 0) cache.delete(h); }, gcTime));
  }

  async function fetchQuery<T>(key: QueryKey, fn: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
    const h = hashKey(key);
    const existing = cache.get(h);
    if (existing?.promise) return existing.promise as Promise<T>;          // DEDUPE: one in-flight request per key

    const entry: QueryState<T> = existing ?? ({ status: 'pending', data: undefined, dataUpdatedAt: 0, error: null, fetchStatus: 'idle', promise: null, observers: new Set() });
    cache.set(h, entry as QueryState<unknown>);
    entry.fetchStatus = 'fetching';
    notify(key);

    const p = fn()
      .then((data) => {
        entry.status = 'success'; entry.data = data; entry.dataUpdatedAt = Date.now(); entry.error = null;   // authoritative: server wins
        entry.promise = null; entry.fetchStatus = 'idle';
        notify(key);
        return data;
      })
      .catch((error: Error) => {
        entry.status = 'error'; entry.error = error; entry.promise = null; entry.fetchStatus = 'idle';
        // KEY RULE: a failed refetch must NOT clear data that is already on screen.
        notify(key);
        throw error;
      });
    entry.promise = p as Promise<unknown>;
    return p;
  }

  const isStale = (key: QueryKey) => { const e = cache.get(hashKey(key)); return !e || Date.now() - e.dataUpdatedAt >= staleTime; };

  return {
    cache,
    fetchQuery,
    hashKey,
    isStale,
    get: <T>(key: QueryKey) => cache.get(hashKey(key)) as QueryState<T> | undefined,
    setData: <T>(key: QueryKey, updater: (old: T | undefined) => T) => {
      const h = hashKey(key);
      const e = cache.get(h) ?? ({ status: 'success', data: undefined, dataUpdatedAt: 0, error: null, fetchStatus: 'idle', promise: null, observers: new Set() });
      e.data = updater(e.data as T | undefined); e.dataUpdatedAt = Date.now(); e.status = 'success';
      cache.set(h, e as QueryState<unknown>); notify(key);
    },
    invalidate: (keyPrefix: QueryKey) => {                                    // prefix invalidation, like RTK's partial match
      const prefix = JSON.stringify(keyPrefix.map(normalise));
      let found = false;
      for (const [h, e] of cache) {
        if (h.startsWith(prefix)) { e.dataUpdatedAt = 0; found = true; e.observers.forEach((fn) => fn()); scheduleGc(h); }
      }
      return found;
    },
  };
}
const normalise = (v: unknown) => (v === undefined || v === null || v === '' ? '__undef' : v);
```
*Edge cases:* hash normalisation is the whole ballgame — `{a: undefined}` and `{}` must be the same entry, and `1` vs `'1'` must not be; an error while refetching keeps the previous `data`; GC only starts when the last observer leaves; `invalidate` matching by string prefix must not match `['a']` against `['ab']` (compare parsed keys, or add a delimiter).

### 5. `useQuery` on top of the cache (~70 lines)

```tsx
export function useQuery<T>(key: QueryKey | null, fn: () => Promise<T>, opts: { staleTime?: number; enabled?: boolean } = {}) {
  const qc = useQueryClient();
  const h = useMemo(() => (key ? hashKey(key) : null), [key]);
  const subscribe = useCallback((cb: () => void) => {
    if (!h) return () => {};
    const e = qc.cache.get(h) ?? ({ observers: new Set() } as any);
    e.observers.add(cb);
    return () => { e.observers.delete(cb); };
  }, [qc, h]);
  const snapshot = useCallback(() => (h ? qc.cache.get(h) : undefined), [qc, h]);

  const state = useSyncExternalStore(subscribe, snapshot, snapshot);
  const enabled = opts.enabled ?? true;

  useEffect(() => {
    if (!h || !enabled) return;
    const entry = qc.cache.get(h);
    const stale = !entry || Date.now() - (entry.dataUpdatedAt ?? 0) >= (opts.staleTime ?? 0);
    if (!entry?.promise && (stale || entry.data === undefined)) { void qc.fetchQuery(key!, fn).catch(() => {}); }
  }, [h, enabled, qc, key, fn, opts.staleTime]);

  return {
    data: state?.data as T | undefined,
    error: state?.error ?? null,
    isPending: state?.data === undefined && (state?.status !== 'error' || state?.promise !== null),
    isFetching: state?.fetchStatus === 'fetching',
    isSuccess: state?.status === 'success',
    refetch: () => qc.fetchQuery(key!, fn),
  };
}
```
*Edge cases:* `fn` must be stable (`useCallback`) or the effect refires forever; the subscribe callback must return an unsubscribe that removes the observer; the initial `useSyncExternalStore` value must be `undefined` on both server and client for the first render, or you get a hydration mismatch; `isPending` is "no data yet", not "a request is in flight".

### 6. Mini SWR with stale-while-revalidate and compare-and-swap mutate (~70 lines)

```ts
export const cache = new Map<string, { data: unknown; fetchedAt: number }>();
const inflight = new Map<string, Promise<unknown>>();
let globalRevalidate: (() => void) | undefined;

export function compareAndSwap(key: string, a: unknown, b: unknown) {          // SWR's internal primitive
  const entry = cache.get(key);
  if (!entry || Object.is(entry.data, a)) { entry && (entry.data = b); return true; }
  return false;                                                                 // a newer value already landed: drop the stale one
}

export async function mutate<T>(key: string, fn: (current: T | undefined) => Promise<T> | T,
  { optimisticData, rollbackOnError = true, revalidate = true } = {}): Promise<T | undefined> {
  const entry = cache.get(key);
  const prev = entry?.data;
  if (optimisticData) { cache.set(key, { data: optimisticData(1) as T, fetchedAt: Date.now() }); globalRevalidate?.(); }
  try {
    const next = await fn(prev as T | undefined);
    cache.set(key, { data: next, fetchedAt: Date.now() });
    return next;
  } catch (err) {
    if (rollbackOnError && prev !== undefined) cache.set(key, { data: prev, fetchedAt: Date.now() });   // RESTORE
    else cache.delete(key);
    globalRevalidate?.();                                                       // and revalidate: the snapshot may be stale
    throw err;
  } finally {
    if (revalidate) { const d = cache.get(key); if (d) await revalidateKey(key, d.data); }               // the write is the truth, then confirm
  }
}

export async function revalidateKey(key: string, keep?: unknown) {
  if (inflight.has(key)) return inflight.get(key);                             // dedupe window
  const p = (fetcher(key) as Promise<unknown>).then((fresh) => { compareAndSwap(key, keep, fresh); return fresh; })
    .catch(() => { /* keep stale data visible; the hook surfaces isValidating/error */ });
  inflight.set(key, p);
  return p;
}

export function useSWR<T>(key: string | null, opts: { revalidateIfStale?: boolean; dedupeMs?: number } = {}) {
  const [, force] = useReducer((n: number) => n + 1, 0);
  const [state, setState] = useState<{ data: T | undefined; validating: boolean; error: Error | null }>(
    () => ({ data: key ? (cache.get(key)?.data as T | undefined) : undefined, validating: false, error: null }));
  useEffect(() => { globalRevalidate = force; return () => { if (globalRevalidate === force) globalRevalidate = undefined; }; }, []);
  useEffect(() => {
    if (!key) return;
    const entry = cache.get(key);
    const stale = !entry || Date.now() - entry.fetchedAt >= (opts.dedupeMs ?? 2000);
    if (!entry || opts.revalidateIfStale !== false) setState((s) => ({ ...s, validating: true }));
    void revalidateKey(key, entry?.data).then((data) => setState({ data: data as T, validating: false, error: null }))
      .catch((e: Error) => setState((s) => ({ ...s, validating: false, error: e })));
  }, [key]);
  return { data: state.data, error: state.error, isValidating: state.validating, mutate: (fn: any) => mutate(key, fn) };
}
```
*Edge cases:* the optimistic callback receives its own argument in SWR (`optimisticData(fetchedData)`) so it can compute from the current value — do not close over `prev` before it exists; `rollbackOnError` restores only if there *was* a previous value, otherwise the entry must be deleted; `revalidate: false` is what makes an optimistic write a CAS write rather than a refetch.

### 7. `useInfiniteQuery` with cursors, dedupe, `maxPages`, and bidirectional loading (~90 lines)

```ts
export function useInfiniteQuery<T, P>(
  key: QueryKey,
  getPage: (pageParam: string | null) => Promise<{ items: T[]; nextCursor: string | null; prevCursor: string | null }>,
  { maxPages = 7, initialPageParam = null }: { maxPages?: number; initialPageParam?: string | null } = {},
) {
  const [pages, setPages] = useState<Array<{ items: T[]; nextCursor: string | null; prevCursor: string | null }>>([]);
  const [status, setStatus] = useState<{ loading: boolean; error: Error | null }>({ loading: true, error: null });
  const cursors = useRef<(string | null)[]>([initialPageParam]);   // cursor per page index
  const seen = useRef(new Set<string>());

  const load = useCallback(async (direction: 'next' | 'prev') => {
    const index = direction === 'next' ? pages.length : 0;
    const param = direction === 'next'
      ? (pages.at(-1)?.nextCursor ?? null)
      : (pages[0]?.prevCursor ?? null);
    if (param === null && pages.length) return;                     // reached the end
    setStatus((s) => ({ ...s, loading: true }));
    try {
      const page = await getPage(param);
      setPages((prev) => {
        const next = direction === 'next' ? [...prev, page] : [page, ...prev];
        return next.length > maxPages ? next.slice(direction === 'next' ? next.length - maxPages : 0, direction === 'next' ? undefined : next.length - maxPages) : next;
      });
      cursors.current[index] = page.nextCursor;
      setStatus({ loading: false, error: null });
    } catch (e) { setStatus({ loading: false, error: e as Error }); }
  }, [pages, getPage, maxPages]);

  const flat = useMemo(() => {                                         // dedupe by id across page boundaries
    const out: T[] = [];
    for (const p of pages) for (const item of p.items) if (!seen.current.has((item as any).id)) { seen.current.add((item as any).id); out.push(item); }
    return out;
  }, [pages]);

  useEffect(() => { if (!pages.length) void load('next'); }, []);       // first page

  return { pages, data: flat, fetchNextPage: () => load('next'), fetchPreviousPage: () => load('prev'),
    hasNextPage: !!pages.length && pages.at(-1)!.nextCursor !== null,
    hasPreviousPage: !!pages.length && pages[0]!.prevCursor !== null,
    isFetchingNextPage: status.loading && pages.length > 0,
    isFetchingPreviousPage: status.loading && false,
    isPending: status.loading && pages.length === 0, error: status.error };
}
```
*Edge cases:* dedupe must happen at merge time, not per page, or an overlap duplicates; `maxPages` sliding in both directions changes the indices your `pageParam` cursor array assumes — keep cursors keyed by page identity, not index; a deleted/edited item can make a cursor return a row you already saw, so dedupe by id is mandatory; the sentinel must be paired with a keyboard-reachable "Load more" button.

### 8. Optimistic mutation with cancel, snapshot, rollback, and settle (~70 lines)

```ts
export function createMutationClient(qc: ReturnType<typeof createQueryCache>) {
  function runMutation<TVars, TData>({
    mutationFn, onMutate, onSuccess, onError, onSettled,
  }: {
    mutationFn: (vars: TVars) => Promise<TData>;
    onMutate?: (vars: TVars) => Promise<{ rollback: () => void } | void>;
    onSuccess?: (data: TData, vars: TVars) => void;
    onError?: (err: Error, vars: TVars, ctx: any) => void;
    onSettled?: (data: TData | undefined, err: Error | undefined, vars: TVars) => void;
  }) {
    return async (vars: TVars) => {
      const ctx = await onMutate?.(vars);                    // 1. cancel + snapshot + optimistic write
      try {
        const data = await mutationFn(vars);
        onSuccess?.(data, vars);                             // 2. adopt the server's authoritative copy
        ctx?.rollback();                                     //    commit the snapshot as obsolete
        onSettled?.(data, undefined, vars);
        return data;
      } catch (err) {
        const e = err as Error;
        onError?.(e, vars, ctx);
        ctx?.rollback();                                     // 3. restore the snapshot
        onSettled?.(undefined, e, vars);                     // 4. invalidate (passed by the caller) even on success
        throw e;
      }
    };
  }

  // A complete usage:
  const toggleTodo = createMutationClient(qc).runMutation<{ id: string; done: boolean }, Todo>({
    mutationFn: ({ id, done }) => api.patch(`/todos/${id}`, { done }),
    onMutate: async ({ id, done }) => {
      qc.invalidate(['todos']);                                            // cancel in-flight reads
      const snapshot = qc.get<Todo[]>(['todos'])?.data;
      qc.setData(['todos'], (old: Todo[] | undefined) => old?.map((t) => (t.id === id ? { ...t, done } : t)));
      return { rollback: () => qc.setData(['todos'], snapshot) };
    },
    onSettled: () => qc.invalidate(['todos']),                             // broad invalidate: correctness over one request
  });
  return { toggleTodo };
}
```
*Edge cases:* the snapshot must be deep-cloned (`structuredClone`) if the cache hands back a live reference; `rollback` must be idempotent and callable from both success and failure; concurrent mutations on the same key need per-key serialisation or a snapshot taken *before* any optimistic write; always send an idempotency key so a retry cannot double-apply.

### 9. Typed, canonicalised URL state with history policy (~60 lines)

```ts
import { z } from 'zod';

export function createUrlState<S extends z.ZodTypeAny>(schema: S, defaults: z.infer<S>) {
  function parse(search: string): z.infer<S> {
    const raw: Record<string, string> = {};
    new URLSearchParams(search).forEach((v, k) => { raw[k] = v; });     // last value wins; arrays are your job
    const parsed = schema.safeParse(raw);
    return parsed.success ? parsed.data : { ...defaults };              // never throw on a hand-edited URL
  }
  function serialise(state: z.infer<S>): string {                        // canonical: omit defaults, fixed key order
    const sp = new URLSearchParams();
    const base = schema.parse(defaults);
    for (const key of Object.keys(defaults as object)) {                // deterministic order = one URL per view
      const v = (state as Record<string, unknown>)[key];
      if (JSON.stringify(v) !== JSON.stringify((base as Record<string, unknown>)[key]) && v !== undefined) {
        sp.set(key, typeof v === 'object' ? JSON.stringify(v) : String(v));
      }
    }
    const s = sp.toString();
    return s ? `?${s}` : '';
  }
  const set = (current: string, patch: Partial<z.infer<S>>, { history = 'replace' } = {}) => {
    const next = serialise({ ...parse(current), ...patch });
    if (history === 'push') history.pushState(null, '', next);
    else history.replaceState(null, '', next);
    window.dispatchEvent(new PopStateEvent('popstate'));                // tell the app its own URL changed
  };
  return { parse, serialise, set };
}
// Usage in a component:
const { q, status, page } = useMemo(() => urlState.parse(location.search), [location.search]);
```
*Edge cases:* `safeParse` + defaults so a bad URL degrades rather than 500s; omitting defaults so `/tasks` and `/tasks?page=1` are the same URL; `replace` for filters and `push` for navigation (4.3.8); dispatching the event so URL-driven queries refetch; do not put secrets in the query string.

### 10. A History-API router with ranked matching, splat, and nested routes (~120 lines)

```ts
export type Segment = { kind: 'static' | 'dynamic' | 'splat' | 'optional'; value: string };
export type RouteDef = { path: string; element: React.ComponentType<any>; children?: RouteDef[]; index?: boolean };

const tokenise = (path: string): Segment[] =>
  path.split('/').filter(Boolean).map((s) =>
    s === '*' ? { kind: 'splat', value: '*' }
    : s.startsWith(':') && s.endsWith('?*') ? { kind: 'optional', value: s.slice(1, -3) }
    : s.startsWith(':') && s.endsWith('?') ? { kind: 'optional', value: s.slice(1, -1) }
    : s.startsWith(':') ? { kind: 'dynamic', value: s.slice(1) }
    : s.endsWith('*') ? { kind: 'splat', value: s.slice(0, -1) }
    : { kind: 'static', value: s });

function rank(segs: Segment[]): number {                                   // lower is more specific
  let score = segs.length * 100;
  for (const s of segs) score += s.kind === 'static' ? 0 : s.kind === 'dynamic' ? 10 : s.kind === 'optional' ? 20 : 90;
  return score;
}

function match(def: RouteDef, pathname: string): { params: Record<string, string>; matched: RouteDef[] } | null {
  const segs = tokenise(pathname);
  const params: Record<string, string> = {};
  const matched: RouteDef[] = [];
  function recurse(defs: RouteDef[], remaining: Segment[], inherited: Record<string, string>): boolean {
    if (!defs.length) return remaining.length === 0;
    const candidates = defs.filter((d) => {
      const s = tokenise(d.path ?? '');
      if (d.index) return remaining.length === 0;
      if (s.length > remaining.length) return false;
      if (s.some((t) => t.kind === 'splat')) return true;
      return s.every((t, i) => t.kind !== 'optional');                    // simplified: optional handled by trying both arities below
    }).sort((a, b) => rank(tokenise(a.path ?? '')) - rank(tokenise(b.path ?? '')));
    for (const d of candidates) {
      const ds = tokenise(d.path ?? '');
      const next = { ...inherited };
      let i = 0;
      for (const t of ds) {
        if (t.kind === 'splat') { next['*'] = remaining.slice(i).map(decodeURIComponent).join('/'); i = remaining.length; break; }
        if (i >= remaining.length) break;
        if (t.kind === 'dynamic') next[t.value] = decodeURIComponent(remaining[i++]);
        else if (t.kind === 'static') { if (remaining[i] !== t.value) { i = -1; break; } i++; }
        else next[t.value] = decodeURIComponent(remaining[i++]);
      }
      if (i === -1) continue;
      const rest = remaining.slice(Math.max(i, 0));
      if (recurse(d.children ?? [], rest, next)) { matched.push(d); return true; }
    }
    if (remaining.length && !defs.length) return false;
    return false;
  }
  if (!recurse([def], segs, params)) return null;
  return { params, matched };
}

// Rendering the matched chain, outermost first, each with an <Outlet>-like child.
function renderChain(matched: RouteDef[], params: Record<string, string>, outlet: React.ReactNode): React.ReactNode {
  return matched.reduceRight<React.ReactNode>((child, def) => {
    const El = def.element;
    return <El key={def.path} params={params} outlet={child} />;            // layout routes render `outlet`
  }, outlet);
}

// Navigation: Link intercepts plain clicks; popstate re-renders. Scroll restoration is 'manual'.
function navigate(to: string, { replace = false } = {}) {
  if (replace) window.history.replaceState(null, '', to); else window.history.pushState(null, '', to);
  window.dispatchEvent(new PopStateEvent('popstate'));
}
window.addEventListener('popstate', () => setState({ pathname: location.pathname, search: location.search }));
```
*Edge cases:* splat swallows the rest and must be last; static segments must match case-sensitively and dynamic ones must be `decodeURIComponent`ed *once* (double-decoding is an XSS vector); matching must try longer/more-static definitions first, so `/invoices/new` beats `/invoices/:id`; a pathless layout route (`path: ''` with children) adds a layout without a URL segment; unknown paths need an explicit splat/404 route or you render nothing.

### 11. Route guards with loaders, actions, redirects and object-level authorisation (~80 lines)

```ts
type Session = { userId: string; permissions: Set<string>; exp: number };

export function createRouteGuards({ getSession, loadUser, forbidden }: {
  getSession: (req: Request) => Promise<Session | null>;
  loadUser: (id: string) => Promise<{ id: string; name: string }>;
  forbidden: { title: string; body: string };
}) {
  const safeNext = (raw: string | null) => {                     // open-redirect guard (4.5.5)
    if (!raw || !raw.startsWith('/') || raw.startsWith('//') || raw.includes('\\')) return '/app';
    return raw;
  };

  async function requireUser(req: Request) {
    const session = await getSession(req);
    if (!session || session.exp * 1000 < Date.now()) {
      const next = encodeURIComponent(new URL(req.url).pathname + new URL(req.url).search);
      throw new Response(null, { status: 302, headers: { Location: `/login?next=${next}` } });   // a thrown Response = a redirect
    }
    return { session, user: await loadUser(session.userId) };
  }

  async function requirePermission(req: Request, permission: string) {
    const ctx = await requireUser(req);
    if (!ctx.session.permissions.has(permission)) throw new Response(JSON.stringify(forbidden), { status: 403, headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' } });
    return ctx;
  }

  async function requireOwnership<T extends { ownerId: string }>(req: Request, resource: T | null) {
    const { user } = await requireUser(req);
    if (!resource) throw new Response(null, { status: 404 });                                        // do not leak existence
    if (resource.ownerId !== user.id) throw new Response(JSON.stringify(forbidden), { status: 403 });   // IDOR defence
    return { user, resource };
  }

  const invoiceLoader = async ({ req, id }: { req: Request; id: string }) => {                        // per-object, not per-role
    const { user } = await requireUser(req);
    const invoice = await db.invoice.findUnique({ where: { id, orgId: user.orgId } });                 // tenant scope in the WHERE clause
    if (!invoice) throw new Response(null, { status: 404 });
    return { invoice };
  };

  const invoiceAction = async (req: Request, id: string) => {
    const form = await req.formData();
    if (form.get('_intent') !== 'archive') throw new Response(null, { status: 400 });
    const { user } = await requirePermission(req, 'invoice:archive');
    const invoice = await db.invoice.findUnique({ where: { id, orgId: user.orgId } });
    if (!invoice) throw new Response(null, { status: 404 });
    await db.invoice.update({ where: { id }, data: { archivedAt: new Date(), archivedBy: user.id } });  // write is scoped too
    return new Response(null, { status: 303, headers: { Location: `/invoices/${id}?tab=history` } });   // 303 -> GET
  };

  return { requireUser, requirePermission, requireOwnership, invoiceLoader, invoiceAction, safeNext };
}

// Rendering a thrown Response: 302 -> navigate; 403 -> the forbidden view; 404 -> notFound.
function routeErrorBoundary(error: unknown) {
  if (error instanceof Response) {
    if (error.status === 403) return <Forbidden />;
    if (error.status === 404) return <NotFound />;
    return <ErrorView />;
  }
  throw error;                                    // real bugs propagate to the nearest error reporter
}
```
*Edge cases:* return 404 rather than 403 when the resource does not exist, so you do not leak existence; tenant scope in the query, not just in the check; 303 (not 302) after a mutation so the browser follows with GET; validate `next`; the guard must run in the *loader*, not in the component, and the API must check again.

### 12. A form controller: uncontrolled registry, schema validation, server errors, field arrays (~130 lines)

```ts
type FieldValues = Record<string, unknown>;
type Errors = Record<string, { message: string; type: 'client' | 'server' }>;
type ValidationResult = { ok: true; values: any } | { ok: false; errors: Errors };

export function createForm<T extends FieldValues>(schema: { safeParse: (v: unknown) => { success: boolean; data?: T; error?: { issues: { path: (string | number)[]; message: string }[] } } }) {
  const fields = new Map<string, HTMLInputElement | HTMLSelectElement | HTMLTextAreaElement>();
  const errors: Errors = {};
  const touched = new Set<string>();
  let values: FieldValues = {};
  let isDirty = false;
  const listeners = new Set<() => void>();
  const emit = () => listeners.forEach((l) => l());
  const setError = (name: string, message: string, type: 'client' | 'server' = 'client') => { errors[name] = { message, type }; emit(); };
  const clearError = (name: string) => { delete errors[name]; emit(); };

  function register(name: string) {
    return {
      name,
      ref: (el: HTMLInputElement | null) => { if (el) fields.set(name, el); else fields.delete(name); },
      onBlur: () => {
        touched.add(name);
        void trigger([name]);                                   // validate ONE field on blur: cheap, accurate
        emit();
      },
      onChange: () => { isDirty = true; if (touched.has(name)) void trigger([name]); },   // re-check a touched field
    };
  }

  function readAll(): FieldValues {                               // pull every registered value from the DOM
    values = {};
    for (const [name, el] of fields) {
      if (el instanceof HTMLInputElement && (el.type === 'checkbox' || el.type === 'radio')) values[name] = el.checked;
      else if (el instanceof HTMLInputElement && el.type === 'number') values[name] = el.value === '' ? undefined : Number(el.value);
      else values[name] = (el as HTMLInputElement).value;
    }
    return values;
  }

  async function trigger(names: string[]): Promise<boolean> {
    const parsed = schema.safeParse(readAll());
    for (const n of Object.keys(errors)) if (errors[n].type === 'client') delete errors[n];
    if (parsed.success) { emit(); return true; }
    for (const issue of parsed.error!.issues) {
      const path = issue.path.join('.') || '_root';
      if (!names || names.includes(path) || path.startsWith(`${names[0]}.`)) errors[path] = { message: issue.message, type: 'client' };
    }
    emit();
    return false;
  }

  async function handleSubmit(e: React.FormEvent, submit: (values: T) => Promise<void>) {
    e.preventDefault();
    for (const n of fields.keys()) touched.add(n);
    if (!(await trigger([]))) { focusFirstError(fields, errors); return; }        // a11y: focus the first invalid field
    try {
      await submit(readAll() as T);
      isDirty = false;
      values = {};
      emit();
    } catch (err) {
      // 422: map server field errors WITHOUT clobbering client state.
      if (err instanceof HttpError && err.status === 422) {
        for (const f of err.body.fields as { path: string; message: string }[]) setError(f.path, f.message, 'server');
        for (const n of Object.keys(err.body.fields as object)) touched.add(n);
      } else setError('_root', 'Something went wrong. Your input was not saved.', 'server');  // form-level message
      emit();
    }
  }

  function reset(next?: Partial<T>) { fields.forEach((el) => { el.value = ''; if (el instanceof HTMLInputElement) el.checked = false; }); Object.keys(errors).forEach((k) => delete errors[k]); touched.clear(); isDirty = false; if (next) Object.entries(next).forEach(([k, v]) => { const el = fields.get(k); if (el) el.value = String(v); }); emit(); }

  return { register, handleSubmit, trigger, setError, clearError, reset,
    getState: () => ({ errors, values, isDirty, isValid: Object.keys(errors).length === 0 }),
    subscribe: (l: () => void) => { listeners.add(l); return () => { listeners.delete(l); }; } };
}

function focusFirstError(fields: Map<string, Element>, errors: Errors) {
  for (const [name] of fields) {
    const el = fields.get(name);
    if (errors[name] && el) { (el as HTMLElement).focus(); (el as HTMLElement).scrollIntoView({ block: 'center', behavior: 'smooth' }); return; }
  }
}
```
*Edge cases:* unchecked checkboxes must be read as `false`, not absent; empty number inputs must not become `0` (a classic `Number('') === 0` bug); server errors must not be wiped by a later client re-validation (`type: 'server'` is protected); focus the first invalid field on a failed submit; `reset()` must clear the DOM too, not just the state; and a form-level (`_root`) error is a message, not a field.

---

# 4.3-4.5 TRACE/OUTPUT DRILLS (18)

These exercise the mechanisms above rather than React render counts. For each: state the input, trace the network/cache/render effect, then explain the result out loud.

**Shared setup for most drills.** `App` renders `<Header user={user}/>` and `<TodoList items={todos} filter={filter}/>`; `todos` comes from `useQuery({ queryKey: ['todos', filter] })` with `staleTime: 30_000`, `gcTime: 10 * 60_000`; the store is the `createStore` from Problem 1.

**Drill 1 — Two components, same key, mount simultaneously.**
Input: `<TodoList/>` in the layout and `<TodoSidebar/>` in the page, both `useQuery(['todos'])`, both mounting on first paint.
Trace: **one** HTTP request. The first `useQuery` creates the entry with `promise` set; the second sees `entry.promise` and returns it (dedupe by key). On resolution both observers are notified in the same batch → **two** renders, not four.
Why: dedupe is keyed on the in-flight promise, not on component identity. Expected answer if someone says "two requests" is wrong.

**Drill 2 — `staleTime: 0`, navigate away and back in 2 seconds.**
Trace: on unmount, the observer count goes to 0 and `scheduleGc` starts a `gcTime` (10 min) timer. On remount, the entry exists with `dataUpdatedAt` older than 0 → a **background refetch fires immediately** while the cached list stays on screen (`isPending: false`, `isFetching: true`). GC does not fire because the entry had observers again before the timer elapsed.
Why: `staleTime` and `gcTime` answer different questions (freshness vs retention). The skeleton must not appear — `isPending` is false.

**Drill 3 — Same, but `staleTime: 60_000`.**
Trace: **zero** requests. `Date.now() - dataUpdatedAt < 60_000` → not stale → no refetch on mount. Cache hit, instant paint, no spinner. This is the "country list" case.
Why: this is the single most useful knob for reducing request volume, and the reason for `staleTime` existing at all.

**Drill 4 — `gcTime: 0` with an unmounted entry.**
Trace: unmount → `clearTimeout` then `setTimeout(…, 0)` → next macrotask the entry is **deleted**. Remount 5 minutes later = a full loading state and a network request; the cached data is gone.
Why: `gcTime: 0` means "no cache between screens". The error you will see is a skeleton flash on every route change.

**Drill 5 — Write the cache directly with `setQueryData(['todos'], fn)`.**
Trace: entry `dataUpdatedAt = now`, `status = 'success'`, observers notified. `isFetching` stays `false`. A later mount with `staleTime: 0` **will still refetch** (data is fresh to observers, but stale by policy).
Why: `setQueryData` writes; it does not mark as "recently validated forever". Only `staleTime: Infinity` stops the refetch.

**Drill 6 — `invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['todos'] })` with three active filters.**
Trace: `['todos','all']`, `['todos','open']`, `['todos','mine']` all match the prefix → all three marked stale and all three refetch. **Three requests for one mutation.** Narrowing to the exact key gives one.
Why: prefix invalidation is a *broadcast*; the number of requests equals the number of live cache entries under the prefix.

**Drill 7 — The stale-write: an optimistic update then a failed mutation.**
Trace: `onMutate` cancels `['todos']`, snapshots, writes `{done: true}`. Server returns 500. `onError` restores the snapshot → the row flips back → `onSettled` invalidates → refetch → the server's value (still `false`) wins. Net: **two visual changes and one request**, and the final state is correct.
Why: rollback alone is insufficient (the snapshot might already have been stale); rollback + invalidate is the correct pair.

**Drill 8 — Two optimistic mutations on the same list, second fails.**
Trace: M1 snapshots `[a,b]`, writes `[a',b]`. M2 snapshots `[a',b]`, writes `[a',b']`. M2 fails → restores `[a',b]` ✓. M1 then fails → restores `[a,b]`, which **reverts M2's successful change**. With `mutationScope` serialising per entity, M2 would not start until M1 settled, so the rollback is correct.
Why: this is the concurrency argument for scoped serialisation or invalidate-on-error instead of rollback.

**Drill 9 — `select` returning a new object.**
Trace: `select: (d) => ({ total: d.items.length })` is safe (TanStack Query memoises select output on the raw data reference). But `useStore(s => ({ total: s.items.length }))` against a plain store is **not** safe: `getSnapshot` returns a new object every call → React sees a changed snapshot on every store notification → infinite re-render ("Maximum update depth exceeded").
Why: the difference is whether anything memoises the selector output. Store selectors are raw; query `select` is memoised.

**Drill 10 — RTK Query `getUsers({ page: 1 })` and `getUsers({ page: 2 })`.**
Trace: two cache entries, two requests, two independent loading states. Now add `getUserById(id)` for an id in page 1: a **third** entry, and it can drift from the list entry (the list is invalidated by `['User','LIST']`, the detail by `['User', id}` — a `updateUser` mutation must invalidate both, or one goes stale).
Why: overlapping-but-different keys are the classic "list and detail disagree" bug (4.4.11 blind spot #4).

**Drill 11 — Infinite query: the user posts a new item at the top while page 1 is loaded.**
Trace: page 2's cursor was computed against the old head. With cursors, page 2 returns rows that were page 1's rows 21–40 **unchanged** (no duplicates) but the new post is never seen until a refetch of page 1. The dedupe set prevents any row appearing twice; `maxPages: 7` keeps memory bounded and drops page 1, so scroll-up triggers `fetchPreviousPage`.
