🔴 Advanced · **Must-know**

**Requirements.** Given a prefix, return ranked suggestions in under ~100 ms perceived; keyboard and screen-reader operable; supports prefix search over a catalogue plus "recent searches" and "popular searches"; optional typeahead segmentation; min query length; rate-limit and error resilience; optional personalisation and cross-device history.

**Scale numbers to say out loud.** 2M daily users, ~20% use search per session, ~8 keystrokes per query → ~1.3M queries/minute peak-ish; catalogue 20M entities. Per keystroke we can afford one request; we cannot afford a request per keystroke *per suggestion source*.

**Client state**
```ts
type SearchState =
  | { kind: 'idle' }
  | { kind: 'typing'; query: string }
  | { kind: 'too-short'; minLength: number }
  | { kind: 'loading'; query: string; requestId: number; stale: Suggestion[] }
  | { kind: 'ready'; query: string; requestId: number; groups: SuggestionGroup[] }
  | { kind: 'error'; query: string; requestId: number; retryable: boolean; stale: Suggestion[] };
```
- One request at a time, identified by an incrementing `requestId`; **responses whose `requestId` is not the latest are discarded** (the canonical stale-response race). Cancel the previous request too — `AbortController` — because cancelling frees the connection and stops wasted server work.
- `query` (raw), `debouncedQuery` (after debounce), and `results` are separate fields. Conflating them is the bug behind "the list flickers while typing".
- Cached responses keyed by normalised query (an LRU of 50) make backspace-free navigation instant and turn a repeat query into no network call.
- Recent searches in a small store (persisted), popular searches from the server, history cross-device if the user is signed in.

**Key flows**
```ts
async function onChange(raw: string) {
  setState({ kind: 'typing', query: raw });
  const q = raw.trim();
  if (q.length < MIN_LENGTH) { abortInFlight(); return setState({ kind: 'too-short', minLength: MIN_LENGTH }); }
  const requestId = ++seq.current;
  debouncedAbort?.abort();
  debouncedAbort = new AbortController();
  await sleep(DEBOUNCE_MS, debouncedAbort.signal);        // 120–180 ms
  const cacheKey = normalise(q);
  const hit = cache.get(cacheKey);
  if (hit) return setState({ kind: 'ready', query: raw, requestId, groups: hit });

  inFlight?.abort();                                        // supersede the previous one
  inFlight = new AbortController();
  try {
    const res = await api.suggest(q, { signal: inFlight.signal });
    if (requestId !== seq.current) return;                 // stale: drop silently
    cache.set(cacheKey, res.groups);
    setState({ kind: 'ready', query: raw, requestId, groups: res.groups });
  } catch (err) {
    if (isAbort(err)) return;
    setState({ kind: 'error', query: raw, requestId, retryable: true, stale: previousGroups });
  }
}
```
**Debounce vs throttle.** Debounce (wait for quiet) is right for "user is still typing". Throttle (max rate) is right for guaranteed freshness, e.g. a live-updating result count. Practical combo: debounce ~150 ms, plus a hard cap so a fast typist cannot queue requests (leading-edge execution for the first keystroke, then debounced). Also: cancel rather than merely ignore, and ignore out-of-order responses — you need **both**.

**Sources and ranking (client's part).** The client composes three sources in one response-shape contract: `prefix` (server, ranked), `recent` (local store, filtered by prefix, capped at 5), `popular` (server, only when the query is empty or the prefix search returns nothing). Ranking is a server concern (Phase 12 → search autocomplete); the client's job is to render the grouping and keep the section order stable so keyboard position does not jump. If you rank client-side, you need the fields the rank depends on (`popularity`, `recency`, `matchQuality`) in the payload — say that explicitly.

**Performance budget.** Debounce 150 ms + request p50 40 ms / p95 120 ms + render ≤ 16 ms → perceived latency under ~300 ms, with the previous result visible while loading (never a blank box). Keep the DOM tiny (cap at 10 suggestions, virtualise only if you show more), debounce *prefetch on hover/arrow* if you want perceived instant selection, and never block input on the request.

**Loading / empty / error.** Loading: keep the last results visible with a subtle progress indicator (no spinner replacing content — it destroys the user's context and causes layout shift). Empty: two different empties — "no matches for *x*" with alternatives (did you mean, browse categories) and "we found nothing recent", which is an invitation to search. Error: if you have stale results, keep them and show a non-blocking inline notice with Retry; if you have nothing, show a Retry affordance; on 429, respect `Retry-After`, stop the auto-retry, and tell the user to slow down rather than spinning. Rate-limit handling is the most-missed flow in interviews.

**Accessibility.** This is the canonical combobox pattern and it is graded strictly:
```jsx
<label id="q-label" htmlFor="q">Search</label>
<input
  id="q" role="combobox" aria-expanded={open} aria-controls="q-listbox"
  aria-autocomplete="list" aria-activedescendant={activeId}
  aria-describedby="q-hint"
  autoComplete="off" spellCheck={false}
/>
<ul id="q-listbox" role="listbox" aria-label="Suggestions">
  {groups.map(g => (
    <li key={g.name} role="presentation">
      <div id={`g-${g.name}`} role="presentation">{g.name}</div>
      <ul role="group" aria-labelledby={`g-${g.name}`}>
        {g.items.map(it => (
          <li key={it.id} id={`opt-${it.id}`} role="option"
              aria-selected={it.id === activeId}>{it.label}</li>
        ))}
      </ul>
    </li>
  ))}
</ul>
```
- **DOM focus stays in the input**; the visual active option is referenced by `aria-activedescendant` (this is the whole point of the pattern, and it is what most candidates get wrong).
- Keyboard: `↓`/`↑` move the active option (with wrap), `Enter` selects, `Esc` closes (and clears the active option), `Tab` accepts-and-moves-on per spec nuance, `Home`/`End` jump, `PgUp`/`PgDn` by 5–10.
- Announce result count with a polite live region (`aria-live="polite"`), and do **not** announce on every keystroke — debounce the announcement or it becomes unusable.
- Results must not be focusable (no `tabindex` on options); `role="option"` inside `role="listbox"`; selected state via `aria-selected`.
- Do not use `aria-selected` for "highlighted" *and* "selected" simultaneously; the active-descendant is the highlight.
- Form submit: `Enter` with no active option submits the raw query (a common, expected behaviour).
- Labels: the input needs a real `<label>`; error and hint text via `aria-describedby`.

**Caching.** Client LRU by normalised query (invalidate on data-version change); server/CDN cache popular-prefix responses briefly (tens of seconds) with stale-while-revalidate; long-lived cache only for "popular searches" (minutes–hours). Recent searches are local, capped, and cleared on consent withdrawal or logout.

**API design.**
```
GET /v1/suggest?q=pho&limit=8&locale=en-GB&session=<opaque>
200 { "version": "...", "groups": [
        { "name": "Products", "items": [{ "id","label","subtitle","score","matchRanges" }] },
        { "name": "Recent", "items": [...] } ] }
429 { "code":"rate_limited", "retryAfterMs": 1500 }
```
- Server must be **authoritative for ranking** and return `matchRanges` so the client can highlight without re-implementing matching.
- Include a data `version`/etag so the client can drop stale caches after a catalogue change.
- Prefix requests should be idempotent, cacheable, and cheap server-side (trie/prefix lookup with a bounded fan-out and a popularity prior — Phase 12).

**Edge cases.** Empty query (show popular + recent); one character (min length 2 unless the catalogue demands 1); whitespace-only; unicode/emoji/normalisation (NFC) and diacritic-insensitive matching; very long paste (cap at 200 chars, debounce once); rapid delete-then-retype; tab-away then back (stale UI); offline (cache last results or a message — never a spinner forever); `Accept-Language`/locale changes; duplicate labels with different ids (key by id, never label); a result that disappeared server-side between render and select (handle 404 on select gracefully); the same query from two tabs; rate limits keyed by IP vs user (design for both).

**Follow-up questions to expect.** How would you rank? (popularity prior + match quality + personalisation + freshness + business boosts.) How do you handle misspellings? (edit distance / phonetic over a prefix trie; or a client-side "did you mean" from a separate service.) How do you test this? (unit tests for the stale-response logic with fake timers; integration test asserting `aria-activedescendant` moves; e2e with a mocked API; load test the ranking service.) How do you measure success? (search-to-click rate, zero-results rate, time-to-first-click, conversion.)

**What breaks at scale.** Ranking service latency dominates p95 (mitigate: per-key request coalescing, a bloom/trie in front of the DB, a cached popularity table); the client's LRU grows unbounded (cap it); the suggestion payload grows (cap `limit`, trim fields); every keystroke from every user multiplies backend QPS (mitigate: debounce + cache + the CDN); and personalisation state grows per user (mitigate: compute a small per-user "affinity vector" offline and merge at query time, never a synchronous join).

---

## 4.6.11.2 Infinite feed

🔴 Advanced · **Must-know**

**Requirements.** Endless scroll of a ranked feed with stable ordering, no duplicates, scroll restoration across navigation, back/forward correctness, prefetch near the sentinel, real empty/end state, analytics that survive batching, bounded memory.

**State model.** The important insight: **items, cursors, and scroll position are three separate concerns and the bugs come from conflating them.**
```ts
type Feed<T> = {
  items: T[];                 // ordered, de-duplicated by id
  pageInfo: { hasNextPage: boolean; endCursor: string | null };
  status: 'initial' | 'loadingNext' | 'loadingPrev' | 'refreshing' | 'done' | 'error';
  error: { code: string; retryable: boolean } | null;
  seen: Set<string>;          // id dedupe; or rely on indexOf for small lists
  scrollAnchors: Map<string, number>;  // id -> offset, for restoration
};
```
- **Cursor pagination, not offset.** `offset` pagination duplicates and skips items when the feed mutates (a post deleted or a new post inserted above you) — the single most important sentence in this design. Cursors are stable but "load older posts" is *prepending*, which needs a *backward* cursor.
- **Dedupe by id on insert**, always. Under realtime insertion, the server may return an item you already have; `mergeById(newItems, existing)` keeps order and uniqueness.
- **Dedupe on re-render** is about keys: use stable `key={item.id}`, never `key={index}` — with prepend-heavy feeds, index keys produce the classic "input resets / items appear to shuffle" bug.

**Key flows.**
- Load next: `IntersectionObserver` on a sentinel with `rootMargin: '800px'` (prefetch before visible), `disabled` while a page is in flight, and a guard that `hasNextPage` is false (otherwise the sentinel re-triggers forever).
- Load previous: a *second* observer near the top with `rootMargin: '-200px'` or an explicit "New posts" button (better a11y, and it avoids surprise jumps).
- **Scroll restoration** is the hard part. Two-part approach: (1) store the id of the top visible item plus its pixel offset when leaving the route; (2) on return, render until that id exists, then `scrollTo(id.offset - measuredOffset)`. Restoring by raw `scrollY` breaks because the DOM height differs while items are still loading. Combine with `history.scrollRestoration = 'manual'` so the browser does not fight you.
```ts
// Restoration: measure after layout, not on mount
useLayoutEffect(() => {
  const anchor = savedAnchor.current; if (!anchor) return;
  const el = listRef.current?.querySelector(`[data-id="${anchor.id}"]`);
  if (!el) return;                                     // item not loaded yet: keep top-loading
  const delta = el.getBoundingClientRect().top - anchor.top;
  if (Math.abs(delta) > 1) listRef.current?.scrollBy(0, delta);
}, [items.length]);
```
- **Prefetch** on `pointerenter` of a visible card (or on `IntersectionObserver` at 50% visibility) to warm the detail API — cheap and invisible.
- **Refresh**: re-fetch page one, merge by id, and diff to decide whether to keep the user's scroll position (if the top items are unchanged, do not move; if they changed, show a "N new posts" pill).

**Performance budget.** Budget for the DOM, not the network: virtualise after ~100–200 rows (windowing with fixed/estimated row heights, or `content-visibility: auto` with `contain-intrinsic-size` as a cheap 80% solution). Target: 60 fps scroll with a long task budget of <50 ms per frame; images lazy below the fold with `width`/`height` set to avoid CLS; the initial page ≤ 20 items so first contentful paint is fast. Keep memory flat — a user scrolling 5,000 items should not retain 5,000 full objects.

**Loading / empty / error.** Skeletons only for the first page (a skeleton per row, matching layout, to keep CLS at 0); for subsequent pages, no spinner — keep the content and load silently. Empty (no items at all): onboarding with a reason to create the first item. End ("you're all caught up"): a visible footer marker so users learn the boundary exists, with a link to something to do. Error mid-scroll: an inline retry bar pinned to the failed position, never a full-screen error that loses the user's place. Refresh error: keep old content and toast.

**Accessibility.** The pattern must announce additions: a polite live region updated with "12 more posts loaded" (throttled — announcing every append is unusable). Provide a non-scroll alternative: a "Load more" button (visually hidden is not enough if it is unreachable — keep it in the DOM, focusable, and honest) so keyboard and switch users are not forced to scroll. Manage focus: when you auto-append, do **not** move focus; when the user explicitly loads more and focus was on the "Load more" button, move focus to the first new item so they continue. Cards must be links with descriptive names, and the feed container gets `role="feed"`/`aria-busy` semantics only if you implement the full pattern, otherwise use a plain list. Respect `prefers-reduced-motion` for scroll animations. Test that a keyboard user can reach and trigger loading without a mouse.

**Caching.** Cache page N's payload (TanStack Query `useInfiniteQuery` or an equivalent keyed by filter set) so navigating back is instant. Invalidate on filter change. The anchor map is ephemeral state kept in a module-level or session store so it survives remounts. Server/CDN: cache the first page for a few seconds (feed freshness is per-user anyway), never cache authenticated personalised pages at the CDN without varying on user.

**API design.**
```
POST /v1/feed/pages        # POST because the sort cursor is long + filters in the body
{ "filter": {…}, "after": "<opaque>", "before": "<opaque>", "limit": 20 }
200 { "items": [...], "pageInfo": { "hasNextPage": true, "endCursor": "…" },
      "hasPreviousPage": true, "startCursor": "…" }
```
Opaque cursors (base64 of a sort key + tiebreaker) hide the schema and let you change ranking without breaking clients. Note the idempotency of paging: `after` is stable, so a retried page returns the same items.

**Edge cases.** A user scrolling while a page loads and then reversing direction (cancel the request); items deleted mid-scroll (filter ids you no longer get from the detail endpoint, or tombstone them); an item edited to change its height (anchor by id, re-measure); a session that expires mid-feed (401 → refresh once, then a full-page re-auth); offline (show cached pages and an offline banner; queue nothing that mutates); rapid filter change (abort in-flight, reset `pageInfo`); the sentinel firing repeatedly because `hasNextPage` was not yet updated; images loading after scroll (reserve aspect ratio).

**Follow-ups to expect.** How do you make it work without JavaScript / with SEO? (Server-rendered paginated list with `?cursor=` in the URL, plus `IntersectionObserver` enhancement — a hybrid.) How do you test scroll restoration? (E2E: scroll, navigate away, navigate back, assert the same top item and offset; deterministic via fixed viewport and `?e2e=1` to disable virtualisation.) How do you handle the ordering with a realtime channel? (Insert with an anchor rule: if a new item's sort key is above the current top, show a "N new" pill; otherwise insert in place, keyed by id.)

**What breaks at scale.** Ranking joins dominate p95 (Phase 12 → news feed); unbounded growth kills scroll performance and memory (virtualise + a cap with an explicit "load older from archive" escape); real-time insertion into a cursor-paginated feed is genuinely hard (the anchor model above); CDN caching is impossible for personalised feeds (accept it, cache the ranking service instead); and analytics batching on every appended page creates a burst pattern that your collector must absorb (batch with `visibilitychange`, cap queue size).

---

## 4.6.11.3 Image carousel / gallery

🔴 Advanced · **Must-know**

**Requirements.** Show N images (thumbnails + main stage), smooth transitions, swipe on touch, keyboard and screen-reader operation, lazy loading, responsive sizing, zoom/lightbox, orientation handling, no CLS, good LCP.

**Layout decisions (state this first).** Thumbnail rail + single large image (best LCP: one image, no layout thrash, easy a11y) vs peeking carousel (multiple images in view: worse LCP, more memory, harder a11y) vs scroll-snap grid (simple, native, excellent swipe, no JS transitions). Say: **thumbs + stage**, because it gives one LCP candidate, predictable layout, and the cheapest keyboard model.

**State.** `{ index, mode: 'idle'|'zoomed', gesture: {startX, deltaX, axis} | null, preload: Set<number>, lightbox: boolean }`. Derive `visible` slides from `index`, and keep preloading as a small LRU set (next, previous, and the first) rather than all of them.

**Key flows.**
- **LCP discipline:** the *first* image is `fetchpriority="high"`, not lazy, with a known `width`/`height` (or `aspect-ratio`) so there is zero CLS. Everything after is `loading="lazy"`. Never lazy-load the hero (3.6).
- **Preload strategy:** prefetch the next and previous at `loading="lazy"`-with-intent once the first is painted; prefetch the next two, not all 50; cancel preloads on rapid navigation (`<link rel="prefetch">` has no cancel, so use `new Image().src` and drop the reference).
```jsx
<img src={images[i].src} width={images[i].w} height={images[i].h}
     srcSet={buildSrcSet(images[i])} sizes="(max-width: 640px) 100vw, 800px"
     alt={images[i].alt}
     fetchPriority={i === 0 ? 'high' : 'auto'}
     loading={i === 0 ? 'eager' : 'lazy'}
     decoding={i === 0 ? 'sync' : 'async'}
     onLoad={() => setLoaded(s => new Set(s).add(i))} />
```
- **Transitions:** animate only `transform`/`opacity`. Slide via a `translate3d` on a track with `will-change` applied just before and removed after (`transform` animation of thousands of images janks; virtualise the track to ±1 slides).
- **Swipe:** `pointerdown/move/up` with a **direction lock** (if |dx| > |dy| then it's a swipe; otherwise let the page scroll), a 15% threshold to commit, and velocity-based flinging. `touch-action: pan-y` on the track so vertical scroll still works. Do not implement swipe with `touchstart/touchmove` if you can use pointer events (mouse + pen + touch unified, and no passive-listener problems).
- **Zoom/lightbox:** open a dialog with the full-resolution image, trap focus, restore focus to the trigger, `Escape` to close, arrow keys to move between images, and a `download`/`open in new tab` escape. Use `<dialog>` or a properly-built modal (4.6.2 primitives) — never a bare fixed div.

**Performance budget.** LCP ≤ 2.5 s on a mid-tier device and 4G: first image ≤ 150 kB at the display size, preloaded, with `fetchpriority`; a transition must hold 60 fps (main-thread JS per transition < 8 ms); memory: decode only visible images (call `img.decode()` deliberately or use `content-visibility`), and revoke object URLs for blob previews; never load full-resolution originals in the stage (serve width-matched variants via `srcset` + a resizing CDN).

**Loading / empty / error.** Per-image skeleton with the exact aspect ratio (no shift); progressive: blurred tiny placeholder (`background-image` of a 20px-wide base64 or an `LQIP`) behind the real image, cross-faded on `load`. Error: a styled placeholder with the file name and a Retry, and the carousel still navigable (one broken image must not break the gallery). Empty: an empty state with an upload affordance. Loading all: skeletons per thumb. Also handle the *slow* network explicitly: a progress affordance for the current image after ~2 s, since `loading="lazy"` gives no progress events.

**Accessibility.** This is the most-audited component in frontend interviews.
- The stage is a **group** with an accessible name and `aria-roledescription="carousel"`, each slide is `role="group"` with `aria-roledescription="slide"` and `aria-label="3 of 12"`.
- The **thumbnails are a tablist or a list of buttons** with `aria-current="true"` for the active one, and each has an accessible name ("View image 3: sunrise over the ridge").
- Keyboard: `←`/`→` change slide; `Home`/`End` first/last; `Escape` closes the lightbox; arrow keys must work when focus is anywhere in the carousel.
- **Auto-advance (if any) must be pausable**, must not steal focus, must respect `prefers-reduced-motion`, and must have a visible pause control — or, better, no auto-advance in a content gallery at all.
- Do not hide the slide list from assistive tech with `aria-hidden` on the only content; if you visually hide inactive slides, they must still be reachable (use `inert`/`hidden` carefully or keep all slides in the DOM with `opacity` + `aria-hidden` on inactive ones and a note that the user can still reach them).
- Images need real `alt` (or `alt=""` if purely decorative); thumbnails get `alt=""` because the button label carries the name.

**Caching.** Immutable, content-hashed image URLs get `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`; the manifest (the list of images) is versioned and cached briefly; the resizing CDN sits in front so variants are cached globally. Local cache: keep decoded images for the visited range in an LRU and evict (browsers manage this, but avoid holding base64 in memory yourself).

**API design.**
```
GET /v1/assets/{id}/variants?w=320,640,1280&format=webp,avif
200 { "srcSet": "…", "sizes": "…", "width","height","blurhash":"…","exifOrientation": 6 }
```
- Return an **aspect ratio** so the client can reserve space before any bytes arrive; include EXIF orientation normalised (or strip it) so the server never serves a rotated-EXIF image that renders sideways.
- Support AVIF/WebP with a fallback chain (`<picture>`); the browser picks, so send one declarative markup contract rather than a JS-driven format switch.

**Edge cases.** Single image (hide all controls); zero images (empty state); portrait vs landscape mixed (reserve per-image ratio, transition the container ratio smoothly or keep a fixed frame with `object-fit: contain` — pick one and be consistent); device pixel ratio 3 (provide 2x–3x variants, don't send 1x only); broken EXIF orientation; very tall panoramas (cap the stage height, `object-fit: contain`); browser zoom to 400% (the stage must reflow, not scroll horizontally — Phase 3.7); RTL (the swipe direction and arrow keys mirror; say this — it is a common miss); slow image decode (show the placeholder, not a frozen layout); images with CORS restrictions if you later want canvas export (`crossOrigin="anonymous"`).

**Follow-ups to expect.** How would you make it work for 200 images? (Virtualise the track, load thumbnails only, virtualise the thumb rail, and fetch the full-size only for the current slide; consider a mosaic/tiles layout with an IntersectionObserver per tile.) How do you make swipe and keyboard coexist? (Pointer events with direction lock, and separate key handling; never a swipe-only API.) How would you test this? (Component tests for keyboard and `aria` state; E2E for swipe with synthetic pointer events; visual regression on the stage in both themes; a11y snapshot of roles.) How do you debug "sometimes the image loads sideways"? (EXIF normalisation server-side, plus a visual regression test with a rotated fixture.)

**What breaks at scale.** 200 images × 12 thumbnails at full resolution is a bandwidth and decode disaster (mitigate: a thumbnail endpoint, virtualisation, and `content-visibility`); decode memory spikes on mobile (mitigate: `img.decode()`, cap concurrent decodes, revoke blobs); 60 fps transitions fail on low-end devices once the track holds many nodes (mitigate: windowing to ±1, `transform` only); and lazy-loading everything means the user perceives an empty gallery (mitigate: eager first, prefetch next two, and a per-thumb placeholder).

---

## 4.6.11.4 Chat UI

🔴 Advanced · **Must-know**

**Requirements.** Real-time bidirectional messaging: send/receive, ordering, presence, typing, read receipts, history pagination upward, resend on failure, offline queue, optimistic UI, media upload, accessibility for live messages, bounded memory, reconnect lifecycle.

**State model — the important part: separate the transport from the conversation.**
```ts
type ChatState = {
  messages: Message[];        // ordered, deduped, with local + confirmed status
  outbox: OutboxItem[];       // pending/failed sends, with client-generated ids
  connection: 'idle'|'connecting'|'online'|'reconnecting'|'offline';
  presence: Record<UserId, { online: boolean; lastSeen: number }>;
  typing: Record<UserId, number>;   // value = expiry ts (auto-expiring!)
  hasMore: boolean;
};
```
- **Optimistic messages** get a `clientId` generated locally and a `status: 'sending' | 'sent' | 'failed'`. On server ack, replace by `clientId` (this is the dedupe rule). Reconnect resends unacked items with the *same* `clientId` so the server can dedupe (idempotency key) — this is the difference between "mostly works" and "works".
- **Ordering** is by server sequence (`seq`, monotonically increasing per conversation), not by client timestamp. Client timestamps are for display only, because clock skew between devices is a guaranteed source of misordering. Sort by `(seq ?? localCreatedAt)`.
- **Typing indicators must expire** (2–4 s TTL, cleared on a timer or on message arrival) or they lie forever.

**Key flows.**
- **WebSocket lifecycle:** `connecting → open → (heartbeat every 25–30 s) → closing → closed → reconnect with exponential backoff + jitter (1 s, 2 s, 4 s … cap 30 s)`. On each successful (re)connect: **resubscribe, then resync** — issue a `GET /messages?since=<lastSeq>` to fetch anything missed while offline, then reconcile the outbox. Resuming a subscription without a resync is the classic "messages from the gap are gone forever" bug. Handle `visibilitychange` (close when hidden on mobile to save battery, reopen on visible), `online`/`offline` events, and page unload (send a presence-leaving beacon).
- **Optimistic send:** append immediately, mark `sending`, POST; on success mark `sent` and stamp `seq`; on failure mark `failed` with a Retry affordance, and keep the text in the input? No — keep it in the message (with retry), which is what real apps do.
- **Offline queue:** persist the outbox to IndexedDB so a refresh does not lose an unsent message; flush on reconnect; show a clear "1 message not sent" affordance rather than silently retrying forever.
- **Read receipts:** server-authoritative (the server records `lastReadSeq` per user per conversation), so it works across devices. Show "Seen" for the other user's last message only when the conversation is open and the document is visible.
- **Scroll behaviour:** stick to the bottom only when the user is already near the bottom; if they scrolled up, show a "jump to latest" pill with an unread count. Never yank the viewport when a message arrives while the user is reading history.
```ts
function shouldStickToBottom(el: HTMLElement) {
  return el.scrollHeight - el.scrollTop - el.clientHeight < 80;   // 80px threshold
}
```
- **Virtualisation is mandatory** for long histories: window the message list, keep the scroll height stable while older messages load above (measure and adjust `scrollTop` by the delta), and preserve focus across prepends.

**Performance budget.** New message render ≤ 8 ms; scroll at 60 fps with 500 virtualised rows; appending 1 message must not re-render the whole list (memoised rows keyed by id, or a virtualiser); WebSocket frame handling on the main thread must be batched (drain the message queue in one `requestAnimationFrame`, not per frame). Incoming bursts (a busy channel) need a batch-apply policy: render the newest 50 immediately, older ones on scroll-back.

**Loading / empty / error states.** Initial history: skeleton rows with realistic heights, then content. Empty conversation: a real empty state ("Say hi to X") — an empty grey box is a bug. Connection lost: a persistent, non-blocking banner ("Reconnecting…") that does not block reading; **never** clear the transcript while reconnecting. Send failed: inline per-message retry. Load-more failure: a retry row pinned in place.

**Accessibility.** Live messages must be announced but not interrupt: an `aria-live="polite"` region containing only the newest message text (append a node, do not re-render a region's whole subtree — that re-announces everything), and use `role="log"`/`role="status"` with `aria-relevant="additions"`. Typing indicators: `aria-live="polite"` with a short message, or better, a `aria-label` on the composer ("X is typing") so it is discoverable without chatter. Every message needs a logical structure: avatar with an accessible name (or `alt=""` when the sender name is adjacent text), a timestamp with `<time dateTime>`, and the sender name as text so it is not colour-only. Keyboard: the log should be focusable (`tabIndex={0}` with `role="log"`) so keyboard users can scroll it; arrow-key navigation between messages is a nice-to-have. Reconnection banners must be `role="status"`. Do not put focus in the transcript on new messages.

**Caching.** Cache recent pages per conversation in memory (TanStack Query with `initialPageParam`), persist the last N messages to IndexedDB for offline read (encrypt if it contains sensitive content — cross-ref 4.6.9), never cache the WebSocket transport. Server history is authoritative; the client cache is a read-through layer.

**API design.**
```
GET  /v1/conversations/{id}/messages?before=<seq>&limit=50
POST /v1/conversations/{id}/messages          # Idempotency-Key: <clientId>
     { "clientId", "text", "attachments": [{ "id", "w","h","mime" }] }
PATCH /v1/conversations/{id}/read             { "seq" }
POST /v1/uploads (presigned, multipart, resumable)
WS   /v1/realtime   → frames:
     { "t":"msg",  "seq", "conv", "clientId", "senderId", "text", "ts" }
     { "t":"ack",  "clientId", "seq" }
     { "t":"presence","userId","state","ts" }
     { "t":"typing","userId","ts" }
     { "t":"receipt","conv","userId","lastReadSeq" }
```
Every frame is `{t, ...}` with a discriminated union — the client switches on `t` exhaustively, so a new frame type is a compile error rather than an ignored message.

**Edge cases.** Two tabs open (both receive the message; dedupe by id so it appears once per tab; presence must count *sessions*, not users, or someone with two tabs looks "online twice"); send while typing indicator is on (clear your own indicator); resending after a long disconnect (server idempotency plus an "edited/failed" state); a 413 for a long message (client-side limit with a counter, server authoritative); attachments uploaded but the message send fails (orphan cleanup job server-side, and an explicit "retry" that reuses the uploaded id — cross-ref Phase 3.9 chunked upload); timezone differences in timestamps (store UTC, render local, label with the full date on day boundaries); message edit/delete propagation (server broadcasts an update; the client patches by id); translation or profanity filtering mutating your own echo (mark the client's copy as `filtered` when the server rewrote it, otherwise the user sees a different message than they sent).

**Follow-ups to expect.** Why WebSockets vs SSE vs long-polling? (Duplex + low overhead; SSE is simpler and auto-reconnects but server→client only; long-polling is universal and works through restrictive proxies.) How do you handle >1 device per user? (Session-scoped presence; per-device read state merged to max.) How do you test realtime? (A local WS server with a scripted timeline; a "chaos" test that kills the socket at each step of a message send; a resync test asserting no gaps after a forced reconnect.) How do you reduce bandwidth? (Binary/protobuf frames, delta updates, `seq` ranges, compress.)

**What breaks at scale.** A single hot room with 10k concurrent sockets needs presence as an approximate count (not a per-user list), fan-out through a broker, and per-connection backpressure (drop slow consumers, buffer bounded). History deep in the conversation makes pagination expensive (cursor by `seq`, index on `(conversationId, seq)`). WebSocket at the edge needs sticky routing or a broker (Phase 12 → chat/WhatsApp). Attachments must leave the WebSocket (presigned upload, then a reference frame) or you will blow memory limits. And iOS backgrounding will kill sockets: design for resync-on-foreground, not for an eternal connection.

---

## 4.6.11.5 Google Docs-style collaborative editor

🔴 Advanced · **Must-know** (asked as "how would you build X" and as the classic "this is a multi-month project" honesty test)

**Scope honesty first.** State it in your first 30 seconds: *this is a multi-month project; in an interview I will scope it to the hard parts and the trade-offs.* Then design within that scope. Interviewers reward this framing more than a confident fake design.

**Requirements (scoped).** Multi-user text editing with live presence, conflict-free convergence, undo/redo, autosave and version history, comments. **Out of scope for the interview answer:** tables, images, equations, drawing, offline-first sync, printing, plugin API, and mobile editing.

**The core problem: convergence.** Two families of algorithms:

| | OT (Operational Transform) | CRDT |
|---|---|---|
| Idea | Transform each operation against concurrent operations so all replicas converge | Represent data as a conflict-free structure; merge is commutative/idempotent, so order does not matter |
| Central piece | A **transformation function** per operation pair, e.g. `t_i(insert(5, 'A')) → insert(5 + |L|)` | A sequence CRDT (RGA) with unique IDs per character |
| Server | **Central authoritative server** that orders operations and broadcasts transformed ops | Any peer; ops can arrive in any order |
| Rich text | Hard (formatting ops interact with ranges; needs per-attribute transforms) | Easier conceptually (per-attribute LWW with proper merges) |
| Cursor/presence | Position transforms are fiddly (needs offset → position mapping) | Native: positions are CRDT positions |
| Offline | Requires server authority and a reconnect protocol | Natural fit (merge on reconnect) |
| Cost to build | Weeks per operation type, and every new op type needs a transform | Months to get a production-quality sequence CRDT |
| Used by | Google Docs (operational transform family) | Figma, and most modern collaborative editors |

**Interview answer structure:** "I would use a CRDT (RGA-style sequence CRDT with unique character IDs and per-attribute last-writer-wins registers), because presence/cursors come for free and offline merging is tractable, accepting a higher memory cost per document (tombstones) and a larger initial build. I would keep a central server for auth, document membership, persistence, and snapshotting, but it would not need to order operations. If we only ever need online collaboration with a single central authority, OT is cheaper to ship."

**Presence and cursors.** Separate channel from document content (never persist it, never version it, never CRDT it). Send throttled (~30–60 Hz, and only on change) relative positions (e.g. `{ leftAnchor: 'char-id-8a3f', offset: 3 }`) rather than absolute offsets — a relative position survives concurrent edits; an absolute offset does not. Decay presence (a cursor with no update for 10 s disappears), and send an explicit `leave` on unload so cursors do not litter the document.

**Undo/redo semantics — the subtle part.** Naive per-user undo stacks break: undoing your own insert after someone else inserted inside it must not delete their text. Correct approach: record undo entries with **intent ranges** ("the run of text I typed") and undo by deleting the surviving characters within that range, not by replaying inverse operations against current state. With a CRDT this is a range deletion over currently-surviving IDs; with OT it is a transformed inverse. Only undo *your own* actions (collaborative editors do not offer "undo what she did"). Keep the stack bounded (50–100 entries) and clear redo on a new local action after an undo.

**Autosave and versioning.** Snapshots, not a save button: server persists updates to an append-only log with a periodic compacted snapshot (e.g. every 100 updates or 10 s of inactivity); version history is created on explicit milestones and on a schedule (keep last N versions plus named versions); every snapshot is independently loadable so recovery does not require replaying the whole log. Compaction requires tombstone GC — which is safe only once all clients have acknowledged past that version (track client clocks and only GC beyond the minimum acknowledged cursor). Include document-level metadata (owner, ACL, created) in the CRDT root so membership changes merge consistently.

**Comments and suggestions.** Model them as **anchored ranges referencing stable CRDT IDs** with a separate comment thread entity; a comment survives concurrent edits because its anchor is relative. Suggestion mode = a proposed range with the replacement text and an author; acceptance applies a real document edit (so it goes through the CRDT and converges) rather than mutating the document behind the CRDT's back. This is a genuinely good design point to say out loud: *suggestions are data; acceptance is an operation.*

**Large-document performance.** Virtualise rendering (only the viewport's text nodes; measure with `IntersectionObserver` on block elements); keep the CRDT document out of the DOM (never render a whole document as DOM nodes); avoid re-creating the editor model on every keystroke; batch remote operations (a microtask or rAF drain, and coalesce remote cursor updates); do not re-parse HTML for the whole document on any local change; consider block-level granularity in the model so a paragraph edit does not dirty the whole document. Budget: typing latency ≤ 50 ms for a 20k-word document on a mid-tier laptop; remote ops applied within 200 ms of arrival.

**Offline.** With a CRDT you can hold updates in IndexedDB and flush on reconnect — but you must handle the hard cases: editing the same region while offline produces a merge that is technically correct and *semantically* ugly (that is the honest limitation), and access revocation while offline (deny on reconnect and roll back). Say both out loud.

**Accessibility (underrated, and a differentiator).** Collaborative editors are hard for screen-reader users because of constant cursor movement. Provide: a keyboard-accessible command palette for formatting (never mouse-only toolbars), an "accessible view" mode that exposes a linearised document, proper roles for the text body (`role="textbox"` with `aria-multiline` on a contenteditable wrapper, or a textarea-backed custom editor for true a11y), announced collaborator events (`aria-live="polite"`: "Alex joined"), and never let remote cursor movements steal focus or scroll the viewport (a classic bug). Contrast remote cursors on selection and don't rely on colour alone (label each collaborator with a name).

**Multiplayer testing.** Deterministic simulation: seed a document, generate N random concurrent operations, apply them in many different orders, and assert convergence (all replicas identical) — this is the test that matters and it is cheap to write with a property-based approach. Then test intent-undo semantics, and test the resync path with an injected packet loss.

**Follow-ups to expect.** CRDT vs OT (above). How do you handle a very large document (10 MB)? (Block granularity, snapshotting, lazy loading of unseen blocks.) How do you implement undo (above). How do you handle a user editing while offline (above). How do you scale the fan-out (server holds the doc state, broadcasts deltas; partition by document on a broker; a snapshotter per doc; Phase 12). How would you scope this for a 3-person team in a quarter (shared text only, no rich formatting, presence as coloured text, no comments; ship single-user editing with CRDT data structures from day one so you do not have to migrate).

**What breaks at scale.** Per-document memory server-side (mitigate: snapshot + delta, GC tombstones beyond the acknowledged cursor); CRDT growth from tombstones and metadata (mitigate: compaction with GC, per-user undo depth limits); a hot document with hundreds of concurrent editors (mitigate: partition by document, per-doc lock-free ordering not required by CRDT but snapshot rate must be tuned, and awareness fan-out is a per-doc pub/sub); the awareness channel at 60 Hz × 500 users = 30k messages/second per document (mitigate: throttle per user, send cursors only for users in the viewport — "cursor interest management", which is what real editors do).

---

## 4.6.11.6 Analytics dashboard

🔴 Advanced · **Must-know**

**Requirements.** Show KPIs and trends over selectable date ranges, filterable by dimension, with drill-down, table + chart visualisation of large datasets, export, optional real-time updates, role-based visibility, and fast enough to be usable at 100k rows.

**Layout and information hierarchy.**
```
┌ Filter bar: [date range] [compare to previous] [segment ▾] [saved views] [export] [live ⟳]
├ KPI row:    Revenue   Orders   AOV   Conv rate        (value + delta vs comparison + sparkline)
├ Charts row: Time series (primary) | Breakdown by dimension (top 8 + "other")
├ Table:      Virtualised, sortable, column-pinned, per-column formatting
└ Detail:     Click a row → drill-down panel (or route) → entity page
```
Hierarchy rules to state: one primary question per view, the KPI row answers "is it normal?", the time series answers "when did it change?", the breakdown answers "which segment?", and the table answers "which rows?". Put the date range and comparison selector in a single consistent place, and make the comparison period explicit (`vs previous 28 days`) — an unlabelled delta is a misreading generator.

**Data-fetching strategy.** Three distinct data shapes, three strategies:
- **KPIs and time series:** one aggregate endpoint with `from`, `to`, `granularity`, `filters[]`, `compare=true`. Aggregates server-side, always. Never ship raw events to the browser to aggregate 100k rows.
- **Tables:** server-side pagination *and* sorting (`sort=revenue.desc`, cursor) — the browser must never hold 100k rows to sort them. Virtualise the 50 rows it does hold.
- **Entity detail:** route-level fetch on drill-down.
Use a query cache keyed by the full filter set, with `staleTime` per surface (KPIs 30 s, tables 60 s, exports server-side). Prefetch the default view on route mount. Parallelise independent KPI requests but cap concurrency; show skeletons per card (so the page is useful before all data arrives) rather than one full-page spinner. Cancel superseded requests when filters change (the stale-response race again) and ignore out-of-order results.

**Caching and consistency.** Warehouse queries are slow (seconds), so: materialised aggregates for common ranges, a precomputed cube/aggregate table per (tenant, day, dimensions), and cache aggressively with a `dataVersion` per tenant-day so all users of a tenant see consistent numbers for a given period (this matters — mismatched numbers between two views destroy trust). Push invalidation when late-arriving data changes a past day, and show "data as of HH:MM" for freshness. Never cache per-user filtered queries across users.

**Timezones and date ranges.** Decisive interview point: the dashboard's timezone is a *product* decision. Store timestamps in UTC; support (a) a fixed reporting timezone (e.g. the tenant's billing timezone, which makes daily totals stable), and (b) a user/browser-local view with an explicit label. Aggregate in the **reporting timezone** server-side, because "yesterday's revenue" in the reporting zone must not shift when a user in another zone looks. Support comparison periods of equal length, "last 28 days", month-to-date, quarter-to-date, and custom ranges with validation (from ≤ to, max span, no future dates). Use the platform `Intl.DateTimeFormat` for labels and an ISO week policy you state explicitly.

**Real-time updates.** Polling vs WebSocket, and the trade-offs:
- **Polling** (e.g. every 30 s on the live tiles only): simple, stateless, works everywhere, slightly stale, costs request volume per viewer.
- **WebSocket/SSE** push: lower latency and cost per viewer at scale, but you now have connection management, ordering, and reconnect (4.6.11.4).
Say the threshold: below ~50 concurrent viewers per tenant, polling is the right call; above that, push. Never push the entire dashboard — push *delta* updates to a few tiles and revalidate the rest. Also: pause updates when the tab is hidden and show "paused" rather than pretending to be live. Aggregate server-side with a small time window (5–10 s buckets) so the update rate is bounded regardless of user count.

**Performance budget for 100k rows.**
- Never transfer 100k rows. Server-side pagination/sorting + aggregation. If a full export is needed, do it server-side as a job (generate a CSV asynchronously, email a signed link) — a 100k-row CSV is not a browser download.
- Virtualise the visible ~40 rows; fixed row height (or measured with `ResizeObserver`) so scrolling is O(visible).
- Keep the chart bounded: aggregate to ≤ 500 points per series (bucket by hour/day as needed) and draw on canvas (or an SVG with a small point count) — a 100k-point SVG path will jank.
- Formatting is a real cost: `Intl.NumberFormat` instances are expensive, so create them once per locale/currency and memoise the formatted values per cell. Precompute formatting in a worker if you must.
- Budget: KPI row visible in < 1 s (cached), table interactive in < 2 s, scroll at 60 fps, filter change reflected in < 1.5 s.
- Isolation: a slow aggregate must not block the filter bar — fetch per card with independent skeletons.

**Loading / empty / error states — this is graded heavily.**
- **Loading:** per-card skeletons with the final layout dimensions (zero CLS); a subtle top progress line for subsequent refetches so the user can keep working with stale-but-labelled data ("updated 12 s ago").
- **Empty:** distinguish *no data yet* (onboarding: "no events in this period", plus "view last 30 days"), *filtered to nothing* (name the filter and offer "clear filters"), and *delayed data* (ETL lag message).
- **Error:** per-card error with Retry and a correlation id; do not blank the whole page because one chart failed. Distinguish permission errors ("ask an admin for access") from query errors (Retry) from timeouts ("this range is too large, narrow it") — and **degrade gracefully**: on a timeout for a heavy range, return a coarser granularity and say so rather than showing nothing.
- **Stale data:** label it. A dashboard that shows old numbers without saying so is worse than one that errors.

**Accessibility.** Charts must be understandable without colour: also encode series by line style/marker/label, provide a data table alternative for the primary chart, and use an accessible name plus a summary ("Revenue rose 12% week over week, driven by the US segment"). Tables: real `<table>` semantics with `<th scope>`, caption, sortable columns exposing `aria-sort`, and focusable interactive cells with names ("Revenue for ACME: $12,340, row 3 of 40"). The live region announces refreshes politely ("Data updated"), never on every cell. Ensure keyboard access to filters, date picker (4.6.2), and drill-down. Respect reduced motion for chart transitions. Contrast: dashboard colour palettes must pass AA on both themes (4.6.1).

**API design.**
```
GET /v1/analytics/kpis?from=&to=&tz=&compare=prev&filters[segment]=us&granularity=day
200 { "dataVersion":"2026-02-01.3", "asOf":"…", "kpis":[{key,value,unit,delta,series:[…]}],
      "cache":{"maxAge":30} }
GET /v1/analytics/rows?from=&to=&sort=revenue.desc&cursor=&limit=50
200 { "rows":[…], "pageInfo":{endCursor,hasNextPage}, "totalCount": 128341 }
POST /v1/analytics/exports { from, to, filters, format } → 202 { jobId }
GET /v1/analytics/exports/{jobId} → { status, url?, expiresAt }
```
Keep the filter grammar consistent (`filters[k]=v` repeated) and validate it server-side; a dashboard API that accepts arbitrary expressions is an injection and a performance hazard.

**Edge cases.** A date range with no traffic; a filter that excludes the user's own row (permissions); a 10× outlier making the y-axis useless (log scale option, or clamp with a note); a metric that changed definition mid-period (annotate the chart at the change date — a very strong answer); a very long tenant name breaking the header; a user with access to no segments (show the "request access" state); timezone of the reporting zone differing from the user's; comparison period containing incomplete data (grey it out); an export of 5M rows (job, not request); a dashboard shared as a URL with filters — encode the filter set in the URL so links are shareable and reproducible (also makes bug reports actionable).

**Follow-ups to expect.** How would you make this real-time? (Push deltas to tiles; polling under a viewer threshold.) How do you handle a metric definition change? (Version the metric, annotate, keep both, backfill.) How do you test this? (Component tests for the empty/error/permission states — most of the value; E2E for the drill-down; a11y checks on the table; visual regression for the KPI row.) How would you optimise for a tenant with 50k users watching one dashboard? (Materialised aggregate + push; a shared per-tenant cached response with a short TTL; compute the aggregation once per 10 s window, not once per viewer.)

**What breaks at scale.** Warehouse fan-out per viewer (mitigate: aggregate tables, a caching layer keyed by (tenant, range, filters), and per-window computation); long ranges over huge fact tables (mitigate: precomputed daily rollups + a max-range policy with coarse bucketing); 100k-row exports (mitigate: async jobs + signed URLs + retention); many concurrent live viewers (mitigate: push fan-out per document, not per viewer); and permissions — per-row filtering in the warehouse (mitigate: mandatory predicate injection server-side, never client-side filtering of a full result set).

---
# PHASE 4.6 INTERVIEW Q&A

| # | Question | Strong answer outline |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | What is a design system, and what is it not? | Versioned, code-encoded decisions + governance; not a Figma file, not just a component library, not a collection of 200 components |
| 2 | Explain token → primitive → component → pattern → page with an example | Value → raw palette → single element → a task composed of elements → a route; primitive has no product knowledge |
| 3 | Why have three tiers of tokens? | Operational: it lets a palette or brand change without touching components, and lets you forbid primitives in product code |
| 4 | How do you stop teams from using primitive tokens directly? | Stylelint/custom lint + a CI grep for raw hex/oklch; design-system-owned token files; code review is not enough |
| 5 | Theme with CSS custom properties — why not Sass variables? | Runtime switching, subtree scoping, no rebuild for a brand, cascade integration; Sass variables are compile-time only |
| 6 | Multi-brand theming: where do overrides live? | Brands override the primitive palette only; semantic layer and components stay identical; loading per-brand primitives at runtime |
| 7 | What does `oklch()` buy you? | Perceptual lightness → predictable contrast, tints/shades in one line, `color-mix` in a perceptual space avoids grey mud; needs fallbacks |
| 8 | Why is contrast asymmetric between light and dark? | Saturation and lightness interact differently per mode; mid-grey and saturated brand colours are the usual failures; test computed contrast in both |
| 9 | Controlled vs uncontrolled components? | Support both: controlled when a value is passed (form libraries), internal state otherwise; both must stay in sync |
| 10 | How do you stop boolean-prop explosion? | Replace booleans with a `variant` union; make contradictory states unrepresentable; prefer slots/compound components |
| 11 | What is an escape hatch and why is removing it harmful? | `className`/style/`data-*` + slot extension points; without them consumers fork the component |
| 12 | Polymorphic `as`: value and cost | Correct semantics and typing for links/buttons; costs type complexity, ref inference, and polymorphic prop type errors |
| 13 | Where do data attributes fit? | Stable styling/inspection hooks (`data-state`, `data-side`) that avoid class specificity wars and are assertable in tests |
| 14 | When do you use compound components? | Multi-part widgets with shared state (dialog, tabs, combobox); keeps sub-parts replaceable and the API readable |
| 15 | How is accessibility built in rather than reviewed in? | Primitives own keyboard patterns, focus management, focus-visible token, reduced motion; a11y unit tests per primitive; manual AT pass per release |
| 16 | Your `Button` needs a `danger` colour and a `large` size. Design the API | `variant` union + `size` union; justify why not `color`+`isDanger`+`size`+`isLarge` (state space, contradictions) |
| 17 | Walk through a breaking change and its deprecation | Semver-major, codemod, dev-only runtime warning, usage scan, parallel majors, remove only at zero usage |
| 18 | When is a big-bang restructure of a design system wrong? | When consumers cannot all migrate at once; use codemods, parallel majors, and a measured usage report |
| 19 | Design system in a monorepo vs many repos? | Monorepo for coordinated changes and CI; multi-repo for isolation/access control; the release/upgrade process is the real cost either way |
| 20 | How do you measure design system success? | Coverage % of UI from primitives, inconsistency count, axe violations, bundle delta per release, deprecation time-to-zero, escape-hatch usage |
| 21 | When is a design system not worth building? | One team, high product churn, ~10 primitives and no patterns; the trigger is cost (third duplicated component), not taste |
| 22 | shadcn/ui vs Radix vs MUI vs Ant vs Chakra? | Distribution model first (source-in-repo vs package), then headless vs styled; ownership of a11y and maintenance follows from that |
| 23 | Why did copy-the-source become popular? | Customisation drops to a diff you control; no release train; the cost is that a11y fixes, React upgrades, and patches become your backlog |
| 24 | Headless vs styled: what are you choosing? | Behaviour ownership vs pixel ownership; speed-to-finished-UI vs visual distinctiveness; a11y burden lands on you in headless |
| 25 | How do you make a third-party component replaceable? | Your own API and types, adapter owns state/a11y, single import site, no leaked types, pin versions, escape hatch without pass-through |
| 26 | Why is `localStorage` wrong for tokens? | Any script on the origin can read it (XSS + third-party tags); use `HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite` cookies with CSRF defence (PHASE 7 → Token Storage) |
| 27 | Storybook: what does it do that unit tests don't? | Renders with real styles so `play` can assert layout/visibility, plus visual regression, a11y, and a human review surface |
| 28 | What is a "story of stories"? | The coverage requirement: one story per state, not one per component — the contract that prevents untested states |
| 29 | How do you make visual regression baselines stable? | Pin browser/platform, bundle fonts, disable animation, seed time/randomness, mask dynamic regions, wait for loaded states before asserting |
| 30 | Why is a `play` function more than a smoke test? | It runs a real user (`userEvent`) against a styled render, so focus order, visibility, and layout are asserted; it doubles as an interaction test in CI |
| 31 | What does the axe addon miss? | Keyboard operability, focus visibility/trapping, announcement quality, name meaningfulness, reflow/zoom, timing |
| 32 | When is Storybook pure overhead? | One team, ~15 components, no designers involved, stories not maintained — and it is overhead if interaction tests live somewhere else |
| 33 | Real motivations for micro-frontends | Independent deploy cadence for multiple teams, deployable ownership, incremental legacy migration, genuinely different domain lifecycles |
| 34 | Costs people underestimate | CSS isolation compromises, dependency duplication, version skew, fragmented observability, composition-level E2E, SEO, N× pipelines |
| 35 | Build-time vs runtime composition | Build-time is simplest and fastest but loses independent deploy; runtime (federation) needs compatibility contracts and a fallback |
| 36 | How is routing owned? | Shell owns top-level routes and auth; MFEs own sub-routes; route manifests must be versioned; unknown-route and remote-failure states must be handled |
| 37 | Which deps must be singletons and why? | React (hooks), router, i18n, token provider, query cache, analytics, auth client — two instances means two histories or broken hooks |
| 38 | Cross-MFE communication channels ranked | URL/route state first, then typed DOM events, then a shared hint store, then shared packages; never import another MFE's internals |
| 39 | Auth across three apps? | Shared `HttpOnly` cookie session on the parent domain, one auth client in the shell, expiry broadcast everywhere, CSRF defence |
| 40 | Shell v2 with remote v1 deployed: failure modes? | Missing API, changed prop contracts, duplicate singletons, event schema drift, cache invalidation; mitigate with additive contracts, remote health checks, previews |
| 41 | Migrate a monolith to micro-frontends: order | Shell last; start with one edge route, extract shared primitives first, establish contracts before MFE #2, sunset the old surface |
| 42 | When should you *not* use micro-frontends? | <20 engineers, one team, coupling-heavy domains, no platform capacity, realtime-coherent products; modular monolith instead |
| 43 | Feature-based vs type-based folders | Feature-based optimises deletion and co-location; type-based enables false reuse and produces dumping grounds; enforce with lint, not review |
| 44 | How do you enforce dependency direction? | `eslint-plugin-boundaries` element types + rules, deep-import bans, `madge --circular` in CI, ratchet down existing violations |
| 45 | Why do barrels hurt? | Larger re-export graphs make tree-shaking harder, blow up watch/build invalidation, hide cycles, and collide silently with `export *` |
| 46 | When does shared code become a package? | ≥2 real consumers, a named owner, independent change cadence, and a stable public API; otherwise duplicate deliberately |
| 47 | How do feature flags belong in the structure? | At one seam, not scattered; both branches tested in CI (matrix); owner + expiry in the definition, enforced by a CI check |
| 48 | Incremental vs big-bang restructure | Refactors must be behaviour-preserving at every commit; move bottom-up with tests, delete the old path in the last PR |
| 49 | What is the single most important Sentry detail? | Release tagging plus source-map upload for the *exact* deployed build; without it, stack traces are unreadable |
| 50 | Unhandled vs handled error capture | Unhandled is the noisy baseline; handled `captureException` at boundaries is the highest-signal data you will ever collect |
| 51 | Context that makes an error actionable | Release, environment, dist, user id, session id, route tag, feature-flag values, breadcrumbs, trace id |
| 52 | Session replay: what must be masked? | All inputs and free text by default, all media, and any unaudited authenticated page; sampling by session; legal review and short retention |
| 53 | How do you stop Sentry noise? | `beforeSend` filtering, custom fingerprints for shared frames, ignore list with written justification, rate-limit per release, split mis-grouped issues |
| 54 | When do you page on an error? | New issues in a release you just shipped, high volume user-affecting regressions, error-budget burn; tickets for the rest |
| 55 | What must never leave the browser? | PII, tokens, cookies, `Authorization` headers, full request bodies, full DOM of authenticated pages |
| 56 | SaaS vs self-hosted Sentry | Start SaaS; self-host only for data residency, legal requirements, or genuine volume; the operational burden is real |
| 57 | Kinds of feature flags and their lifetimes | Release (expires), kill switch (never expires), experiment (ends in a decision), entitlement (long-lived) |
| 58 | How do you bucket a percentage rollout? | Deterministic hash of a stable id salted with the flag key — sticky across sessions and devices, independent across experiments |
| 59 | Where should a flag be evaluated? | Server/edge for anything affecting markup or data (correct HTML, authoritative, cache-correct); client for presentation-only, untrusted |
| 60 | Assignment vs exposure | Assignment = evaluated; exposure = actually rendered; analyse on exposure with a stable id, or results are diluted and biased |
| 61 | Expand/contract for flags | Add new → dual write → backfill → switch reads behind a flag → verify → remove old; a flag does not migrate data |
| 62 | Three flag-induced bugs | Client/server skew (mismatch + hydration), stale flags cached in a bundle or long-TTL storage, branch sharing mutable state with the stale side |
| 63 | Flags and caching | HTML containing a variant must vary the CDN key on the cookie/header; flag-affected payloads must be in the cache key |
| 64 | Event naming convention and schema versioning | Past-tense object.action (or verb-noun) consistently; `schema_version` in every event; never repurpose a name |
| 65 | Client vs server analytics | Client for interaction and CWV, server for truth (revenue, entitlements); join on a shared id and reconcile |
| 66 | What is a tracking plan? | Question → decision → funnel → event spec (name, typed props, owner, version) → storage/retention; owner approves new events |
| 67 | Identity stitching and where it breaks | Anonymous id → identify on login; breaks on shared devices, logout, clearing cookies, multi-account households — say so honestly |
| 68 | Consent: where must the gate live? | In the collector itself, not at call sites, so no event can escape; withdrawable as easily as given; DNT alone is not sufficient legally |
| 69 | Why are events lost on navigation? | `fetch` without `keepalive` is aborted on unload; use `sendBeacon` on `visibilitychange`/`pagehide`, batched |
| 70 | How do you keep analytics out of INP? | Fire-and-forget, never awaited; flush on visibility change; keep the collector off the interaction path and small |
| 71 | Where does React's escaping stop? | `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, URLs in `href`, prop spreading, user-authored rich text, direct DOM APIs, third-party embeds, `postMessage` |
| 72 | Safe pipeline for user markdown | `html: false` parser → AST → React elements; no raw HTML passthrough; normalise at render time; sanitize at output, not write |
| 73 | Why does `unsafe-inline` defeat CSP? | Injected inline scripts become allowed, so CSP no longer blocks the thing it exists to block |
| 74 | Nonce CSP with `strict-dynamic` | Per-response random nonce on every script tag; `strict-dynamic` lets nonced scripts load further scripts (including from CDNs) so third parties stay without `unsafe-*` |
| 75 | How do you roll out CSP safely? | Report-only with a monitored violation endpoint, fix violations, then enforce; document inline-style policy explicitly |
| 76 | Risk of a third-party tag and how to contain it | Arbitrary JS with your origin's privileges; mitigate by not loading it, isolating it in a sandboxed cross-origin iframe with `postMessage`, allow-listing in CSP, and monitoring |
| 77 | "We hide the button, so it's secure" — your response | Client authorization is UX only; the server must enforce per object (PHASE 7 → Broken Access Control/IDOR); IDs in URLs are untrusted |
| 78 | `X-Frame-Options` vs `frame-ancestors` | Legacy binary header vs CSP directive that can express an origin allowlist; use `frame-ancestors` (and ship `SAMEORIGIN` for old browsers) |
| 79 | Why is `VITE_API_KEY` not a secret? | It is inlined into the public bundle at build time and readable by anyone; scope/rotate/limit server-side, keep secrets off the client |
| 80 | Dependency confusion vs typosquatting | Public package shadowing an internal name (fix: scope + registry config + `npm ci`) vs a name spelled like a popular package (fix: verify provenance before adding) |
| 81 | What can `npm audit` not tell you? | Reachability, real exploitability, transitive business criticality; it has high noise, so allow-list with expiry and add reachability-aware review |
| 82 | Handling a 429 | Client: read `Retry-After`, message the user, queue/back off, never tight-loop; server: authoritative per-identity limits with a shared store |
| 83 | Vue's reactivity in two sentences | `Proxy`-based `ref`/`reactive`; reads inside a reactive scope are tracked, writes re-run that scope; component render tracks its dynamic dependencies |
| 84 | What does Angular's DI give you that React lacks? | Hierarchical, tree-shakable DI with explicit provider scoping — a convention that removes per-component decisions at large team size |
| 85 | Why does Svelte have no virtual DOM? | The compiler emits direct DOM mutations, so no diffing runtime and a much smaller bundle; trade-off is less dynamic flexibility |
| 86 | Why React? Honest answer | Ecosystem, hiring, RN leverage, unidirectional data flow, concurrent features — plus the honest counter-cases (Vue reactivity, Svelte runtime, Angular conventions) |
| 87 | Which framework for a low-bandwidth market? | Requirements first: bundle/runtime budget and team skill; Vue or Svelte for lean bundles; do not pay React's ecosystem cost you will not use |
| 88 | Design a typeahead: debounce and cancellation | Debounce ~150 ms, `AbortController` to cancel, request-id check to drop stale responses, LRU query cache, keep last results visible while loading |
| 89 | Typeahead a11y essentials | `role="combobox"` with `aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`, `aria-activedescendant`; focus stays in the input; `listbox`/`option`/`group`; polite live region for counts |
| 90 | Why cursors over offsets in a feed? | Mutating data makes offsets duplicate and skip rows; cursors are stable, and you need a backward cursor to prepend |
| 91 | Scroll restoration that actually works | Anchor by item id + offset (not `scrollY`), render until the anchor exists, then measure and adjust; disable native restoration |
| 92 | Optimistic updates: when and how | Idempotent writes with a client id, dedupe by id on ack, rollback only when safe (no server state), never for irreversible actions |
| 93 | Virtualisation trade-offs | Row-height measurement vs fixed height, scroll-anchor instability, accessibility of off-screen content, find-in-page over virtualised rows |
| 94 | Offline-first: which operations need conflict handling? | Only writes; reads come from the local replica. Need idempotency keys, tombstones, and a documented conflict policy per entity |
| 95 | Chat: resync after reconnect | Re-subscribe *and* `GET messages?since=<lastSeq>`, then reconcile the outbox using stable client ids |
| 96 | Chat a11y essentials | `role="log"` with `aria-relevant="additions"`, polite announcements for new messages only, focusable log, timestamps with `<time>`, typing indicator discoverable not chatty |
| 97 | CRDT vs OT | CRDT: commutative/idempotent merge, free cursors and offline, higher build cost and tombstone overhead. OT: cheaper to ship online, central authority, transforms per op pair are fiddly |
| 98 | Undo in a collaborative editor | Undo *your own* actions by deleting surviving characters in the recorded intent range, not by inverse operations against current state |
| 99 | Dashboard performance at 100k rows | Aggregate server-side, paginate/sort server-side, virtualise ~40 rows, bucket charts to ≤500 points, memoise `Intl` formatters |
| 100 | Dashboard caching and consistency | Materialised aggregates, a tenant-day `dataVersion` so all views agree, short TTLs, "updated HH:MM" labels, never cache per-user filtered results across users |

---

# PHASE 4.6 MACHINE-CODING AND PROJECT DRILLS

| # | Task | What it proves |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Token pipeline: JSON tokens → CSS custom properties + TS object + docs table, light/dark | You understand tiers, one source of truth, and multi-target output (4.6.1) |
| 2 | Design-system CI guard: lint forbidding primitive tokens and raw hex in product code | Boundaries enforced mechanically, not by review |
| 3 | `Button`/`Input`/`Dialog` on your tokens with controlled + uncontrolled input, `as`, ref forwarding, `data-*` | Component API design and escape hatches, not just markup |
| 4 | Third-party wrapper: own API + adapter over a date picker, plus a "swap the library" test | Replaceability, no type leakage, state/a11y ownership (4.6.2) |
| 5 | Storybook setup with args/argTypes, autodocs, `play` focus test, axe, visual regression | The full component-workflow pipeline including CI gating (4.6.3) |
| 6 | Story rot detector: CI job that fails on stories referencing removed/deprecated props | You know documentation drift is a build problem, not a habit |
| 7 | Micro-frontend shell: two routes, two independently built remotes, shared React singleton, typed event bus | Runtime composition, shared dependency negotiation, cross-app messaging (4.6.4) |
| 8 | Compatibility contract demo: remote v2 against shell v1, with a versioned prop/event surface | You can design for skew, not just for the happy path |
| 9 | Enforce module boundaries with `eslint-plugin-boundaries` + `madge --circular` in CI | Structure as enforceable architecture (4.6.5) |
| 10 | Incremental restructure: move a messy `utils/` tree into features, green at every commit | You can actually execute a refactor safely |
| 11 | Sentry integration: release tagging, CI source-map upload, `beforeSend` scrubbing, flag tags, custom fingerprint | Production-grade error pipeline rather than a drop-in SDK (4.6.6) |
| 12 | Flag SDK: deterministic hash bucketing, exposure logger with dedupe, typed definitions with owner/expiry, CI expiry check | You think about flags as lifecycle-managed, measured infrastructure (4.6.7) |
| 13 | Consent-gated analytics collector with `sendBeacon` batching, schema validation, debug mode, and a navigation-loss test | Correctness under navigation + governance (4.6.8) |
| 14 | CSP hardening: nonce-based strict CSP in report-only mode + violation endpoint, third-party tag allowed | You can deploy security without breaking production (4.6.9) |
| 15 | Typeahead with debounce, `AbortController`, stale-response guard, LRU cache, and full combobox a11y | The canonical frontend system-design build (4.6.11.1) |
| 16 | Infinite feed with cursor pagination (forward + backward), id dedupe, sentinel prefetch, and id-anchored scroll restoration | Pagination correctness and viewport control (4.6.11.2) |
| 17 | Virtualised gallery of 200 images: stage + thumb rail, `srcset`, LCP discipline, swipe + keyboard, lightbox dialog | Performance and accessibility together, the two things interviewers watch |
| 18 | Chat UI: WebSocket client with heartbeat, exponential backoff, resync-on-reconnect, optimistic send with idempotent `clientId`, offline outbox in IndexedDB | Real-time reliability, the hardest frontend reliability problem (4.6.11.4) |
| 19 | CRDT convergence test: seed a document, apply N ops in shuffled orders, assert identical replicas | You understand why convergence is testable and how OT/CRDT differ |
| 20 | Analytics dashboard: aggregated KPI endpoint, server-side sort/pagination, virtualised table, timezone-correct ranges, per-card loading/error states | Data fetching, caching, and state design at product scale (4.6.11.6) |
| 21 | A11y hardening pass on your own three components: full keyboard, focus restore, live regions, axe + manual AT | You can find and fix real defects, not just pass a scanner (Phase 3.7) |
| 22 | Bundle rescue: analyse, split, replace a heavy dependency, add size-limit guardrails in CI | Performance as engineering discipline, not a one-off (3.8) |

**Rubric (each 0-2):** correctness and edge cases · architecture and boundaries (dependency direction, own API surface) · accessibility (keyboard, focus, semantics, live regions) · performance (budgets stated and met, rendering and bundle cost) · resilience and operations (cancellation, retries, error/empty/loading states, observability, flags).

---

# PHASE 4.6 DEBUGGING SCENARIOS

Each scenario follows **Symptoms → Likely causes → Investigation → Tools → Fix → Prevention.** The pattern to demonstrate out loud is that you *form a hypothesis from the timeline*, not from the code.

## Scenario 1 — The design system is leaking into the app (and the app into the design system)

**Symptoms:** `Button` renders correctly in Storybook but wrong in production: wrong padding, missing hover, dark-mode colours wrong on one screen only. Meanwhile one feature team has added `!important` overrides in eight files and the CSS bundle grew 40 kB.

**Likely causes:** (a) a feature bypassed the semantic tier and referenced a primitive token, so its surface ignores dark mode; (b) a global CSS reset leaked out of a feature's co-located file (a `.container` or `button {}` rule in a global stylesheet); (c) specificity conflict with a utility class that lands *after* the component stylesheet; (d) an un-scoped style in the copied `components/ui` file; (e) a hard-coded `#hex` introduced under deadline.

**Investigation:** DevTools computed styles on the failing element → walk up the cascade to find which rule wins (in the "Styles" pane, the winning rule is struck-through-in-reverse: greyed-out rules lost); check whether the value came from a custom property and what that property resolves to; `grep -rn "#[0-9a-f]\{3,6\}" src/` and `grep -rn "var(--blue\|var(--gray" src/`; check stylesheet order in the network panel; reproduce in Storybook with the *production* bundle (`vite build && vite preview`) — if it only breaks in the built app, suspect order or tree-shaken CSS.

**Tools:** browser computed styles + CSS custom-property inspector, stylelint (`declaration-property-value-disallowed-list`, `no-descending-specificity`), the CI token check from 4.6.1, CSS Modules scoping review, bundle analyzer for the growth, visual regression on the design-system stories.

**Fix:** replace primitive references with semantic tokens; move feature styles into CSS Modules/scoped blocks; remove global element selectors from component CSS (use `:where()` for zero-specificity defaults so utilities can override intentionally); delete the `!important` overrides by adding the missing token/size instead; if the CSS grew, find which component pulled a new global sheet.

**Prevention:** the tier lint plus the raw-colour CI check (4.6.1), visual regression on every primitive in both themes, `size-limit` on the UI package, a documented rule that primitives never style bare elements globally, and code ownership of `packages/ui`.

## Scenario 2 — Micro-frontend version skew after a shell release

**Symptoms:** after the shell released v2, users who load a cached remote v1 see a blank `/reports` route, a console error about an undefined context, and duplicated React warning ("Invalid hook call" in one route only). Some users are fine.

**Likely causes:** the shell v2 stopped providing a context the remote v1 consumed; the shared singleton negotiation loaded two React copies (version ranges did not overlap); the shell's route manifest changed a prefix the remote still claims; a shared component prop changed type without being additive.

**Investigation:** reproduce with the shell pinned at v2 and the remote pinned at v1 (preview environment with pinned versions — this is why you have one); check `window` for two React instances (`__REACT_DEVTOOLS_GLOBAL_HOOK__` registration count, or a `Symbol.for('react')`/duplicate-key warning in the console); read the console error's stack for which context is missing; check the release manifests: which shell version introduced/removed the context; correlate the affected-user rate with the shell release adoption curve and with cache age.

**Tools:** preview environment with pinned remote versions, federation `shared` config with `requiredVersion`/`singleton`, Sentry release + `mf_version` tags (4.6.6), `postMessage` event schema versioning, cross-repo usage dashboards.

**Fix:** restore the context in the shell with a deprecation shim (additive, not a breaking change) and keep both majors alive; tighten `requiredVersion` with `singleton: true` and an explicit range so a mismatch throws loudly at load instead of silently duplicating; add a remote-load timeout + retry + a user-visible fallback panel so a broken remote never blanks a route.

**Prevention:** a compatibility contract (additive-only within a major), versioned route manifests and event schemas, an integration test matrix in CI that boots the current shell against N-1 and N-2 remotes, and a per-MFE `version` tag on every error and metric so skew is detectable from telemetry.

## Scenario 3 — Storybook drift

**Symptoms:** Storybook stories render, but several fail at runtime in the app: a story uses a prop removed last month, another depends on a mock that no longer exists, and one component has no story for its `loading` state.

**Likely causes:** props were removed without updating stories; stories import `dev/mockData` which was deleted or moved; new states were added without stories; a story passes a value that no longer type-checks because `args` are loosely typed.

**Investigation:** run the storybook build and the test-runner in CI and read failures (this should already fail — if it does not, the pipeline is the bug); `madge`/grep for imports of deleted modules; for the missing state, diff the component's props union against the exported story list.

**Tools:** Storybook test-runner with `play` functions, `vitest`/`jest` with stories as a test suite, a custom check comparing `component.__docgenInfo` props to story usage, an ESLint `no-restricted-syntax` rule flagging deprecated props inside `*.stories.*`, Storybook 8/9 component-docs metadata as the source of the prop table.
