**Fix:** fix the failing stories immediately; type story args strictly (`satisfies Story`) so a prop change becomes a type error; add missing state stories; delete stories in the same PR as prop removals (add a CI check for props removed but still referenced).

**Prevention:** run interaction tests on every story in CI (rot becomes a failing test, not a lie), gate new components on having at least the required state stories, keep stories next to the component in the same directory and PR, and make `npm run storybook:build` a required status check.

## Scenario 4 — Sentry noise and false positives

**Symptoms:** 3,000 events/day, ~40 distinct issues, and 8 people have muted the channel. Two issues have 900 events each from completely different bugs. The top issues are all `ChunkLoadError` from the weekly deploy window.

**Likely causes:** shared frames (router wrapper, API client) create mega-issues; `beforeSend` does not filter infrastructure noise; deploy-time chunk-load errors are already handled by a retry boundary so they are duplicate reports; ad blockers/network extensions trigger abort errors; one user's corrupted session generates thousands of events.

**Investigation:** group by `fingerprint` and by `release`; for a mega-issue, look at the *variety* of messages and breadcrumbs under the same top frame; check `tags` for user/session concentration (a single `session.id` dominating = a loop, not a bug); check the timestamp distribution against deploy windows; ask whether the retry boundary actually caught the error (if it did, `captureException` there is redundant).

**Tools:** `beforeSend`/`beforeBreadcrumb` filters, custom `fingerprint`, per-issue rate limiting, Sentry inbound filters and ignore lists, `denyUrls` for known extensions, dashboards for event volume per issue and per user.

**Fix:** filter handled-and-recovered errors (`ChunkLoadError` inside the retry boundary returns `null`); split the mega-issues with a custom fingerprint including the operation type; rate-limit repeats after N occurrences per issue; add a session-based circuit breaker client-side (`Sentry.getCurrentHub().bindClient` skip after 50 events in a session); delete noise issues with a written justification.

**Prevention:** adopt "alert on new issues in a release you just shipped" instead of "alert on any event"; keep the ignore list small and reviewed; write a definition of a *handled* error (anything you recovered from does not get reported, or is reported with `level: 'log'`); measure alert-to-action ratio quarterly and delete channels that nobody acts on.

## Scenario 5 — Flag-induced flash of wrong content

**Symptoms:** ~3% of users briefly see the old checkout layout, then it swaps to the new one. No errors. Analytics show a spike in `checkout_step_viewed` with the new step names and then a repeat with old names.

**Likely causes:** the server rendered with the flag **off** and the client evaluated it **on** (hydration mismatch, then React re-renders); the flag value is cached in `localStorage` with a long TTL while the server uses live config; the CDN cached the HTML for the other variant; the bundle was built with a build-time flag default baked in (`VITE_FLAG_*`) while production config says otherwise.

**Investigation:** reproduce with a cold cache and with the flag flipped in a preview environment; inspect the served HTML (`curl -H 'Cookie: …'`) for the variant markup and compare with the client-evaluated value; check the CDN `Vary`/`Cache-Control` headers and the cache key; check whether the flag value appears as a literal in the built JS (`grep` the bundle); compare affected-user rate with rollout percentage (3% ≈ the off-cohort).

**Tools:** `curl`/`view-source` plus `Cache-Control` inspection, the bundle grep for inlined values, hydration-mismatch console warnings, flag exposure events with variant (4.6.8), Sentry `flags` tag on errors, a synthetic check from two regions.

**Fix:** evaluate the variant on the server for anything markup-affecting and pass it down as initial state (`<div data-variant="b">` or a serialised config), so the first client render matches; remove build-time inlining of flag values; reduce the flag TTL to minutes and revalidate on route change; add the variant (and, for personalised pages, the cookie/header) to the CDN cache key; until fixed, force the flag to 0% to stop the flash.

**Prevention:** a rule that flags affecting markup/data are server-evaluated and included in the cache key; a test that asserts server HTML equals the hydrated tree for both variants; a CI check that fails if a flag name appears as an inlined literal in a build; a dashboard of variant distribution by page view (the flash shows up as a duplicate-funnel artifact).

## Scenario 6 — Analytics events lost on navigation

**Symptoms:** the funnel's last-step counts are consistently ~15% below backend order counts, and the gap grows with page load. Events sent via `fetch` in the last 800 ms before a navigation vanish. No errors appear.

**Likely causes:** `fetch` without `keepalive` is aborted when the document unloads; flushing on a route change *after* navigation started; events flushed on `beforeunload` (unreliable on mobile and when a bfcache/kill occurs); no `visibilitychange` handler; a single large payload exceeding `sendBeacon`'s size limit; an ad blocker blocking the collector domain (check with the request failing silently).

**Investigation:** DevTools network with "Preserve log" and throttled network → watch the pending request during a navigation; compare client-side counts to a backend count for the same window (quantify the gap per page type); check whether the failing requests appear as `(canceled)`; test with the beacon path and see the gap close.

**Tools:** `navigator.sendBeacon` on `visibilitychange`/`pagehide`, `fetch(..., { keepalive: true })` fallback, batching with a queue cap, `sendBeacon` size limits (~64 kB in practice → split), a local collector debug mode that prints instead of sending, a backend reconciliation report comparing counts by day.

**Fix:** switch the flush path to `sendBeacon` with the `keepalive` fallback, flush on `visibilitychange` (not just unload), batch to stay under the cap, and treat analytics as fire-and-forget everywhere (never `await`).

**Prevention:** a "no event lost on navigation" integration test (fire an event, navigate immediately, assert arrival at a local collector); a nightly reconciliation dashboard comparing client events to server truth with an alert on >5% divergence; document the collector contract (batching, cap, retry policy) and keep it in the analytics PRD rather than tribal knowledge.

## Scenario 7 — CSP breaks a third-party widget

**Symptoms:** after enabling `script-src 'self'` in report-only mode, the console shows hundreds of violations for `https://cdn.vendor.com/widget.js`; the support chat widget and the payments iframe do not load. The payments flow also fails in production where CSP became enforcing.

**Likely causes:** the widget loads scripts from a CDN that is not in `script-src`; it injects inline scripts and inline styles (needs `'unsafe-inline'` or a nonce it cannot have); it creates a blob/worker URL (`blob:` missing from `script-src`); `frame-src`/`child-src` is missing for the iframe; `connect-src` missing for the widget's API; the nonce was applied to your scripts but the CSP used hashes instead, so nothing is trusted.

**Investigation:** read the CSP violation reports (they include `blocked-URI`, `violated-directive`, and `sample`/line numbers via the `report-sample` directive); enumerate the vendor's real requirements from their documentation and from the network panel on an un-CSP'd page; check `report-sample` so violations include the offending snippet; verify the nonce length/entropy and that it is per-response, not per-session.

**Tools:** `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` + a report collector (4.6.8 collector or the Sentry breadcrumb pipeline), `strict-dynamic` with a nonce on your entry script (lets trusted scripts load further scripts, including from other origins, without listing them), `frame-src`/`child-src` for the iframe origin, `report-sample` for actionable violations, SRI where the vendor serves immutable assets.

**Fix:** nonce-based `script-src 'nonce-…' 'strict-dynamic'` so the vendor's dynamically loaded scripts are allowed as descendants of your trusted bundle; add the vendor's data/API origins to `connect-src`, `img-src`, `font-src`; add the iframe origin to `frame-src`; keep `'unsafe-inline'` **out** of `script-src` — if the widget needs inline scripts, isolate it in a sandboxed cross-origin iframe and communicate by `postMessage`.

**Prevention:** a CSP change checklist (report-only → monitor → fix → enforce) with an owner; vendor contracts reviewed before a widget is added; a standing rule that any new third-party script requires a CSP change reviewed by whoever owns security; and alerts on unexpected violation volume (an attacker's injected script shows up as a new violation pattern).

## Scenario 8 — The bundle grew 30% after a dependency update

**Symptoms:** after a routine dependency bump in CI, the main chunk grows 118 kB → 154 kB gzipped and LCP regresses ~400 ms. Nothing in our source changed.

**Likely causes:** the new version added a side-effectful module or removed `sideEffects` metadata from `package.json` (bundlers then keep everything); a deep import changed to a barrel path (`pkg` → `pkg/dist/index.js`), pulling the whole library; the dependency now ships a different module format (CJS instead of ESM) which disables tree-shaking; a peer dependency pulled in a polyfill that used to be excluded; the update changed a peer range so a duplicate copy is now installed; a build config change (minifier settings, `manualChunks`, or dropping `treeshake.moduleSideEffects`).

**Investigation:** run the bundler's visualizer and diff the module list against the previous build (`--stats` diff or two analyzer uploads); check the lockfile diff for duplicates (`npm ls <pkg>` shows two versions); inspect the dependency's `package.json` for `module`/`exports`/`sideEffects`; test by pinning back one version at a time (`npm i pkg@prev`) to confirm causation; check whether a `manualChunks` split disappeared (a chunk rename can make everything appear merged into the entry).

**Tools:** bundle analyzer / stats diff, `npm ls` for duplicates, `size-limit` or a bundle-size CI gate with a diff comment on PRs, `arethetypeswrong`-style module checks, a lockfile-diff review step in CI, `vite build --mode production` reproducibility.

**Fix:** restore the deep import path if the update broke tree-shaking; add an explicit `sideEffects` allow-list in your own package if your metadata is the problem; keep a size-limit budget so a 30% increase fails the PR; if the library genuinely grew, replace it or lazy-load the affected feature.

**Prevention:** dependency updates in small PRs with the size gate in CI; a `CODEOWNERS`-reviewed lockfile; add a `renovate`/dependabot config with grouped minor updates so a single major does not land silently; monitor the trend, not just the threshold (a slow 2%/month creep is the real story).

## Scenario 9 — Memory growth from session replay / long sessions

**Symptoms:** a dashboard tab left open for hours grows to 4 GB, then crashes. Growth correlates with session replay enabled and with the feed being scrolled for a long time.

**Likely causes:** replay buffers unbounded DOM mutation records; the feed keeps appending items to state without ever trimming (no cap, no eviction); an `IntersectionObserver`/listener registered per appended row and never disconnected; a `Map`/`Set` accumulating every id ever seen with no eviction; an object-URL leak (blobs created per image preview and never `revokeObjectURL`'d); an event listener on `window` added per component instance (Phase 4.1 effect-cleanup bug) — cross-ref 4.1.7.

**Investigation:** DevTools heap snapshots over time (take at minute 1 and minute 30; compare retained size and the biggest constructors); `performance.memory`/`chrome://memory` for a rough trend; Performance panel for allocation spikes during scroll; search the code for `addEventListener`, `new Map()`, `new Set()`, `URL.createObjectURL` without a matching cleanup; check replay configuration for retention/buffer limits.

**Tools:** heap snapshots and the "allocation timeline", Chrome Task Manager (JS heap per tab), `performance.mark`/`measure` around scroll, `Map`/`Set` with an LRU wrapper, `WeakMap` for per-object metadata, `AbortController` for listener groups.

**Fix:** cap the feed state (retain a window plus a cursor to load older from the server) and evict the `seen` set alongside; virtualise so off-screen rows unmount (this is the real fix for unbounded growth); pass an `AbortController` signal to every listener and abort on cleanup; `revokeObjectURL` on preview teardown; cap replay sampling and buffer size and turn off replay for the heaviest routes.

**Prevention:** a memory budget test — an e2e test that scrolls for N minutes and asserts JS heap stays under a threshold (this catches regressions permanently); ESLint rule or a lint check flagging `addEventListener` without an `AbortSignal`; cap state sizes by design (a feed has a max window, a cache has an LRU); document the eviction policy for every long-lived collection.

## Scenario 10 — Keyboard trap in a modal from a library

**Symptoms:** a third-party dialog can be opened but not exited with the keyboard; Tab cycles inside it forever, and Escape does nothing on one route. Users with screen readers report being "trapped".

**Likely causes:** the library's dialog is nested inside another focus trap (our app modal + their modal), so the outer trap re-grabs focus after the inner closes; the `Escape` handler is attached to the document and another handler calls `stopPropagation`; the component is rendered in a portal *outside* the app's dialog root, so our global trap sees no focus and keeps cycling; `inert`/`aria-hidden` on the shell is applied while the dialog is open (from an a11y utility) and never removed, and something falls back to a brute-force trap.

**Investigation:** keyboard-only walkthrough with the focus visible (Tab/Shift+Tab/Escape) and note where focus goes; `document.activeElement` logged after each Tab to see the cycle; check for nested `[role="dialog"]` elements in the DOM (`document.querySelectorAll('[role="dialog"]').length`); search the code for `addEventListener('keydown'` on document and for `preventDefault` on Tab; check whether the library expects a `container`/`disableOutsideClickEvents`/`disableEscapeKeyDown` option — most headless libraries expose exactly these escape hatches.

**Tools:** our own dialog primitive built on a well-tested headless library (4.6.2), with `onCloseAutoFocus`, `disableEscapeKeyDown`, `trapFocus` options; a debug utility that renders a visible focus ring and logs focus moves; axe (which will **not** catch this — say so); a keyboard-only e2e test per modal pattern; Playwright's `keyboard.press('Tab')` loops in a test to assert you can escape.

**Fix:** wrap the third-party dialog in our own primitive so *we* own the trap contract (open/close, focus restore, Escape, scroll lock, nested-dialog stack); keep a modal stack so only the top-most dialog traps focus; ensure the portal container is inside the app root or that the app's trap is suspended while any dialog is open; explicitly restore focus to the trigger on close.

**Prevention:** a single owned dialog primitive (4.6.1/4.6.2) with the escape hatches documented; a `ModalStackProvider` that suspends outer traps; a mandatory keyboard e2e per dialog story using Storybook `play` (4.6.3) — `Tab` until focus leaves, `Escape` closes, focus returns to the trigger; an a11y review checklist item that cannot be waived for third-party overlays; and remember the axe limit: automated tools catch roughly a third of these.

**Bonus scenario — "the page is slow only for users":** field RUM shows LCP p75 = 4.1 s on a fast laptop lab test at 300 ms (4.6.8); difference is user geography (long TTFB), a third-party tag blocking the main thread, and a hero image with no `srcset` on a high-DPR screen. Investigation: compare lab vs field, then correlate with TTFB breakdown and the long-task attribution in the Performance panel and the trace (4.6.6). Fix: `preconnect` to the API origin, defer the tag to after interaction/idle, and serve a width-matched AVIF hero with `fetchpriority="high"`; prevent with RUM alerts on p75 LCP tied to a release (4.6.6 alert routing) and a per-route performance budget in CI.

---

# PHASE 4 FINAL READINESS CHECKLIST

Phase-wide consolidation across React mechanics (4.1), patterns and ecosystem (4.2), styling/forms/routing/state/data (4.3–4.5), and architecture/system design (4.6). Deliberately *not* a copy of any single section's checklist.

**React mechanics (4.1)**
- [ ] I can explain the render/commit phases and name what actually triggers a re-render
- [ ] I can debug stale closures and dependency arrays by reading the effect, not by trial and error
- [ ] I know when an effect is unnecessary and can restate it as an event handler
- [ ] I can explain `useMemo`/`useCallback` trade-offs, and when memoisation costs more than it saves
- [ ] I can place `useLayoutEffect` vs `useEffect` correctly (measurement/paint vs async side effects)
- [ ] I understand Context re-render propagation and how to split value and dispatch
- [ ] I can use Suspense, transitions, `useDeferredValue`, and error boundaries correctly
- [ ] I know React 19's Actions, `use`, `useOptimistic`, `useFormStatus`, and what the compiler changes

**Patterns, styling, forms, routing, state (4.2–4.5)**
- [ ] I can choose composition vs configuration, and know the compound-component trade-off
- [ ] I can design a CSS architecture (Modules/Tailwind/tokens) and say how it interacts with specificity
- [ ] I can build a form with a schema shared by client and server, and handle server-side validation errors
- [ ] I can explain routing, layouts, and code splitting at route boundaries, and lazy-load correctly
- [ ] I can separate server state from client state and choose a data-fetching strategy with caching and invalidation
- [ ] I can implement optimistic updates with rollback, and know when they are wrong
- [ ] I can write accessible tabs, dialogs, comboboxes, and menus from the ARIA patterns

**Design system and component ecosystem (4.6.1–4.6.3)**
- [ ] I can explain the token tiers and enforce the primitive/semantic boundary in CI
- [ ] I can implement light/dark and multi-brand theming with custom properties and `oklch`/`color-mix`
- [ ] I can design a component API (controlled/uncontrolled, `as`, slots, escape hatch, data attributes) and justify it
- [ ] I can compare shadcn/ui, Radix, MUI, Ant, Chakra on distribution model and ownership
- [ ] I can wrap a third-party component so it is replaceable with no type leakage
- [ ] I can build a Storybook with args, docs, `play` interaction tests, a11y gating, and stable visual baselines
- [ ] I can state when a design system is not worth building

**Architecture and boundaries (4.6.4–4.6.5)**
- [ ] I can give real motivations for micro-frontends and enumerate the costs honestly
- [ ] I can compare build-time, runtime, server-side, and iframe integration styles
- [ ] I can define a shell/router ownership model, shared singletons, and cross-MFE communication channels
- [ ] I can design a compatibility contract and handle version skew with a fallback
- [ ] I can lay out feature-based layering and enforce it with lint rules plus cycle detection
- [ ] I can explain barrel trade-offs, path-alias consistency, and when shared code becomes a package
- [ ] I can plan an incremental restructure that is green at every commit

**Production operations (4.6.6–4.6.8)**
- [ ] I can set up error monitoring with release tagging, source maps, scrubbing, and flag tags
- [ ] I know what must be masked in session replay and who approves it
- [ ] I can decide what to alert on and connect errors to SLOs
- [ ] I can implement flags with deterministic bucketing, exposure events, and a lifecycle with owner + expiry
- [ ] I can name three flag-induced bugs and the check that prevents each
- [ ] I can design an event taxonomy, a tracking plan, and a consent-gated collector that loses no events on navigation
- [ ] I can explain identity stitching, consent, sampling, and when client events are insufficient

**Security (4.6.9)**
- [ ] I can name every realistic React XSS sink and fix it (sanitise at render, allowlist URLs, no prop spreading)
- [ ] I can write a `postMessage` handler with origin and shape validation
- [ ] I can explain nonce-based strict CSP with `strict-dynamic` and roll it out report-only first
- [ ] I know why `localStorage` tokens and build-time env "secrets" are failures, and the correct alternatives
- [ ] I can state that client authorization is UX only, and where enforcement really lives
- [ ] I can clickjack-check with `frame-ancestors`, sandbox a third-party iframe, and justify each token
- [ ] I understand dependency confusion vs typosquatting, `npm ci`, lockfile integrity, and the limits of `npm audit`
- [ ] I know which security headers matter for a frontend and what breaks if they are wrong

**Framework breadth (4.6.10)**
- [ ] I can explain Vue's `ref`/`reactive`/`computed` model, Angular's zones/signals/DI, and Svelte's runes/compile-time approach
- [ ] I can name the framework-agnostic concepts that transfer to any framework
- [ ] I can answer "why React" with an honest, requirements-driven answer including counter-cases
- [ ] I can read a framework-agnostic job posting and infer frontend maturity

**Frontend system design (4.6.11)**
- [ ] I can run the 10-step skeleton: requirements, state, flows, budget, states, a11y, caching, API, edge cases, scale
- [ ] I can build a typeahead with debounce, cancellation, stale-response protection, and a correct combobox pattern
- [ ] I can build an infinite feed with cursor pagination, dedupe, and id-anchored scroll restoration
- [ ] I can design a gallery with LCP discipline, `srcset`, swipe plus keyboard, and a lightbox dialog
- [ ] I can build a chat client with heartbeat, backoff, resync-on-reconnect, and idempotent optimistic send
- [ ] I can explain OT vs CRDT, awareness, intent-based undo, and why collaborative editing is a multi-month project
- [ ] I can design an analytics dashboard with server-side aggregation, virtualised tables, and timezone-correct ranges
- [ ] I can state a performance budget and the scale limit for each of the six systems

**Cross-cutting judgement**
- [ ] For any frontend change I can name the a11y, performance, security, and observability consequences before writing code
- [ ] I can pick the right level of abstraction: build, adopt, or wrap — with a reason and an exit plan
- [ ] I can scope a migration incrementally so production is never blocked on a rewrite
- [ ] I can defend a decision with trade-offs and name the failure scenario I am trying to avoid

---

# PHASE 4 COMPLETE

Phase 4 delivered, in order: React mechanics (rendering, hooks, effects, re-render discipline), React patterns and ecosystem (composition, forms, suspense, error boundaries, React 19), the rest of the frontend stack (styling, forms, routing, state and server data), and this part — the architecture layer: design systems, component libraries, Storybook, micro-frontends, project boundaries, error monitoring, feature flags, analytics, frontend security, framework breadth, and six full frontend system designs.

The through-line of the phase: **make change cheap and failure visible.** Structure and design systems reduce the cost of the next change; monitoring, flags, analytics, and tracing reduce the time to detect that the last change broke something.

**Next up, Phase 5: Backend Foundations.** Reply **"next"** and I'll deliver:
- Node.js and the event loop in production (canonical entry: 3.3.6), streams and backpressure
- HTTP servers and frameworks, routing, middleware, and request lifecycles
- Express and Fastify, error handling, validation, and API design conventions
- Authentication and authorization in depth (canonical entry: PHASE 7 → Auth Mechanisms)
- Working with databases, ORMs, query performance, and transactions
- Caching layers, queues, background jobs, and scheduling
- Testing, logging, and error handling for backend services

# PHASE 4: Frontend Frameworks

### React Concepts ✅
- ✅ Component lifecycle, State management, Hooks, Context API, Performance optimization, Server components, Custom hooks
- ➕ Virtual DOM and reconciliation, Keys, Fiber architecture, Rendering and re-render causes, `useEffect` pitfalls, `useMemo`/`useCallback`/`memo` trade-offs, `useReducer`, `useRef`, `useLayoutEffect`, `useTransition`/`useDeferredValue`, Suspense and error boundaries, Controlled vs uncontrolled components, Portals, Refs and `forwardRef`, Compound components, HOCs vs render props, React 19 features (Actions, `use`, `useOptimistic`, `useFormStatus`), Concurrent rendering, Strict Mode, Hydration

### 🆕 Next.js and Meta-Frameworks (🔴)
- App Router vs Pages Router, SSR vs SSG vs ISR vs CSR, React Server Components vs Client Components, Server Actions, Data fetching and caching, Streaming and Suspense, Route handlers, Middleware, Image/font optimization, Edge vs Node runtime, SEO and metadata, Deployment (Vercel, self-hosting), Awareness of Remix, Astro, Nuxt

### State Management ✅
- ✅ Redux Toolkit, Zustand, MobX, Recoil, Context usage, Local storage, Server state
- ➕ When to use which, Redux middleware (thunk, saga), Selectors and reselect, Normalized state, Jotai, XState and state machines, URL as state, Optimistic updates

### 🆕 Data Fetching and Forms (🟡)
- TanStack Query / SWR (caching, stale-while-revalidate, invalidation), Pagination and infinite scroll, Polling vs WebSockets, React Hook Form / Formik, Schema validation (Zod/Yup), File upload UX, Error and loading states

### Routing Strategies ✅
- ✅ Client routing, Dynamic segments, Nested routes, Route guards, Lazy loading, Navigation hooks, History API
- ➕ File-based routing, Hash vs history mode, Protected routes with auth, Deep linking, Scroll restoration, Parallel and intercepting routes

### 🆕 Frontend Architecture and Ecosystem (🔴)
- Design systems and component libraries (shadcn, MUI, Radix), Storybook, Micro-frontends, Folder structure, Error monitoring (Sentry), Feature flags, Analytics, Frontend security, Awareness of Vue, Angular, Svelte, "Why React?" answers
- Frontend system design: autocomplete, infinite feed, image carousel, chat UI, Google Docs-style editor, dashboard
